# Material del Capítulo 17 — Cómo pensar

Este archivo reúne TODO el material obligatorio del capítulo, ordenado por sección y clúster según `_libro/04_arquitectura.md`. Las unidades aparecen completas (copiadas de `_libro/02_unidades/`).


# SECCIÓN 17.1 — Stupid thinking y genius thinking


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-30: Opening your mind (be water)
- idea: Abrir la mente es pensar en grande: alejarse, olvidar las preocupaciones y notar más de la vida. El autor lo sintió dormido escuchando a Alan Watts y recomienda seguir leyendo los libros que producen ese efecto. Bruce Lee ("be water") en la lectura del autor: la taza es la perspectiva que estructura la consciencia. Las experiencias cumbre ocurren con una taza adecuada (flow) o sin taza (apertura total, meditación). Lo ideal es poder pasar de una taza a otra y entenderlas sin "freezing into one cup". Desafiar las creencias comunes le permitió al autor superar métodos mientras otros seguían igual tres años después, mientras quien adopta creencias ajenas (cold outreach como única vía) obtiene resultados "effective but not the best". Wittgenstein da la imagen de enseñarle a la mosca a salir de la botella: la mente es una botella y los humanos quieren romper un caparazón tras otro.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-083
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Bruce Lee's "be water": the cup is the perspective that structures consciousness; peak experiences come from either a fitting cup (flow) or no cup (openness).
- **desarrollo:** "Empty your mind, be formless, shapeless like water; you put water into a cup, it becomes the cup" (Bruce Lee; "there's more to that quote"). Everyone knows it, but how many sat with it for a practical application? His interpretation: the cup is the perspective, and your consciousness is structured by it. Why it works: peak experiences are most notable in two forms — (a) flow state, when consciousness is ordered and you have clarity: "the proper structure or cup to fill your consciousness with"; (b) when you open your mind beyond all limitation, form or belief, as in meditation, where you try to increase the gap between thoughts and open yourself to the world. Later extension: you should be "like water and the cup and the water": able to flow and pour into different cups and understand them without "freezing into one cup."
- **ejemplos:** Flow; meditation.
- **cita:** "the cup is the perspective and your Consciousness is being structured by that perspective"
- **terminos:** be water, the cup, peak experiences, freezing into one cup
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Bruce Lee
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-073
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 13:49–14:33, 19:34, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna

### U-022-006
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Wittgenstein: the aim of philosophy is "to show the fly the way out of the fly bottle."
- **desarrollo:** The author quotes Ludwig Wittgenstein ("if I said that correctly"): "What is your aim in philosophy? To show the fly the way out of the fly-bottle." He uses it as the framing image for the whole video: you have a project, goal or idea for a life (making millions, marrying a person you couldn't see yourself without, losing weight, learning a skill, anything that leads to growth), and the mind is a bottle you need to find the way out of. He connects this to his claim that "humans have an intrinsic desire to break out of one shell then the next then the next."

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-30: Selective skepticism
- idea: "Selective skepticism": la respuesta inconsciente, automática y programada ante oportunidades ("sounds like a scam", "I can't do that because", "what if it doesn't work"); es selectiva porque se aplica a las oportunidades y no al camino por defecto. Mecanismo: la mente no puede ver un futuro en que le va bien; las creencias la encierran en "this small little bubble" y la atención no trasciende el problema → vida mediocre. Ver todo como estafa retrasa tu desarrollo años: las escuelas enseñan formas obsoletas de ganar dinero y toda idea nueva parece blasfemia. Tres tipos de consumidores de información: los distraídos, los curiosos y los atrapados en contenido que dice que la información nueva (cursos, coaching) es mala; no tiene sentido descartar "an epoch of existence" por una mala experiencia. Señal relacionada de mente cerrada: "vision avoidance", reprimir el potencial para evitar la incertidumbre (gratificación instantánea vs visión: "hurts now and feels good later").
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-002
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** Selective skepticism: the automatic, programmed rejection of opportunities ("sounds like a scam", "I can't do that because", "what if it doesn't work").
- **desarrollo:** The author names "selective skepticism" as "a critical flaw in their self-development." When people are presented with opportunities to start a business, meet new people or change their life as a whole, their "unconscious automatic and programmed response" is: "sounds like a scam", "I can't do that because…", "but what if it doesn't work". Mechanism: their mind cannot see a potential future where they do well; their beliefs trap where their thoughts can go "in this small little bubble"; their attention "can't transcend the problem." Consequence chain: if your decisions create your future, and your mind can't see the good future, this only leads to a mediocre life. It is "selective" because the skepticism is applied to opportunities, not to the default path.
- **ejemplos:** People online presented with opportunities who respond "sounds like a scam."
- **cita:** "Their mind can't see a potential future where they do well; their beliefs trap where their thoughts can go in this small little bubble."
- **terminos:** selective skepticism; small little bubble; transcend the problem
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** learned helplessness (U-022-001)
- **fuente:** How To Take Back Control Of Your Life With Strategic Thinking.md [0:52] (2025-03-16)
- **tension:** ninguna

### U-007-163
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Three groups of information consumers: the distracted, the curious, and those who think new information is bad
- **desarrollo:** (1) The distracted: most people use online information to distract themselves via argument, mainstream news, or flavor-of-the-day drama. (2) The curious: they observe this is a trap leading them down the wrong path and become curious about the opposite direction: finding good information to build their life with. (3) Those trapped in a negative cycle of consuming content telling them new information is bad, meaning courses, coaching, info products. Dan asks them to come with an open mind, or not watch: it makes no sense to "write off an epoch of existence, the age of information" because of one bad experience with a sleazy marketer or "snake oil salesman" (he mentions Liver King, though not a course seller, and avoids drama).
- **ejemplos:** Liver King as snake-oil reference
- **cita:** "It doesn't make sense that you would write off an epoch of existence, the age of information, just because you had one bad experience"
- **terminos:** the distracted; the curious
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-007-160
- **fuente:** You Have A $100,000 Product In Your Head (One Person Business Series).md, 3:39-6:32, 2022-12-04
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-082
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Sign 4 of close-mindedness: vision avoidance — repressing potential to avoid uncertainty.
- **desarrollo:** A vision isn't clear in an instant; you need a constant reminder, and it's an uncertain path. People don't want uncertainty, so they repress their potential: they know there's something more but never go down "the rabbit hole of curiosity," never break out of what they're told to learn or what's handed to them, scrolling memes rather than searching for something that can help. Instant gratification "feels good now but hurts later," whereas your vision is the opposite: "it hurts now and feels good later." You want to flip this switch.
- **ejemplos:** Scrolling memes.
- **cita:** "the instant gratification of that feels good now but hurts later when your vision is the opposite where it hurts now and feels good later"
- **terminos:** vision avoidance, rabbit hole of curiosity
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-071
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 13:06–13:49, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01b-31: Stages and practice of open-mindedness
- idea: La apertura mental tiene etapas: creencia → rechazo → ver verdad en todo (religión: única salvación → control mental → verdades profundas leídas metafóricamente; negocios: freelance es el mejor modelo → los modelos son estafas → todos tienen verdades útiles); más apertura, más éxito (inteligencia, creatividad, menos reactividad, relaciones, ideas, navegar la política). Práctica: al iniciar tu camino la mente reaccionará a todo lo que amenace tu cosmovisión; cuando notes una reacción negativa ("las redes son tóxicas", "los negocios son para quien ya tiene dinero", "escribir es para académicos"), "call yourself out" y piensa todas las perspectivas, no solo la que desalienta la acción. Entrenamiento: Schmachtenberger ("be a deep generalist") y The Kybalion para notar principios universales en la realidad (big picture thinking). "Collecting consciousness" mediante "transferring consciousness": buscar entender, no juzgar (depredador y presa, el lector, el conductor que te cerró el paso). Las enseñanzas solo "registran" en tu consciencia cuando estás en la situación vital y con la intención que las hace relevantes (The Power of Now "too woo-woo").
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna (ambigüedad interna sobre el orden de las etapas; ver Evolución)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-084
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Open-mindedness comes in stages: belief → rejection → seeing truth in all (religion and business examples).
- **desarrollo:** Example "in no specific order" (as he says, though presented sequentially): Religion — stage 1: religion is the only path to salvation; stage 2: religion is mind control; stage 3: religion has deep truths when interpreted metaphorically. Business — stage 1: freelancing is the best business model; stage 2: business models are scams; stage 3: all business models hold truths that can be used to improve the one aligned with your interests. More open-minded stages lead to more success. Benefits of open-mindedness: deeper intelligence, heightened creativity, less reactivity, fulfilling relationships, novel content ideas (for creators), artful navigation of polarizing domains like politics. (Includes a self-aware plug for Digital Economics as a holistic business course.)
- **ejemplos:** Religion and business model stages.
- **cita:** "stage three you believe that all business models hold truths that can be used to improve the one that aligns with your interests"
- **terminos:** stages of open-mindedness
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-080
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 14:33–16:19, 2023-01-22

### U-017-091
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** "Collecting consciousness" via "transferring consciousness": seek to understand, not judge — like a predator assuming its prey's moves.
- **desarrollo:** Practical step 3. To collect consciousness you must transfer consciousness and "seek to understand, not judge." Not unique to humans: a predator can transfer consciousness into its prey and assume its next moves — not planning, just knowing what mistakes it'll make and positioning itself. If a predator can, a marketer or you can. Marketers and content creators are good at it because they understand human psychology; study human psychology, marketing and sales to understand others' minds and help them beneficially with your content, product, services, life's work. Writer hack (taught in The 2 Hour Writer): transfer consciousness into the reader/viewer — will they engage and share? If not, how can you make it better? Everyday example: the driver who cut you off — assume something positive: what if their mother is in the hospital and they're trying to reach the exit, while you worry about your comfort ("oh my God I'm gonna die")?
- **ejemplos:** Predator and prey; reader engagement; driver who cut you off.
- **cita:** "to collect Consciousness you need to be able to transfer Consciousness and seek to understand not judge"
- **terminos:** collecting consciousness, transferring consciousness
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-079
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 21:56–24:08, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-004
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Teachings only register when you are in the life situation that makes them relevant (why some find a book "too woo-woo")
- **desarrollo:** The author is big on goal setting "whether you are aware of that goal or not or whether you keep it at the top of mind or not" — you need something to aim towards. Many people he has recommended The Power of Now to say it is "too woo-woo," "too spiritual," or they didn't understand it. His explanation: they are not in the situation in life where the teachings will register in their awareness in a way that would make them change their behavior to get the outcome they want. Intention is what makes a teaching stand out and become usable. Implication: the same content can be life-changing or meaningless depending on the reader's current situation and aim.
- **ejemplos:** People reacting to The Power of Now as too spiritual.
- **cita:** "they're not in the situation in life where the teachings will register in their awareness"
- **terminos:** intention, register in their awareness
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-003
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 1:39–3:09, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T02b-39: Pre-rational → rational → post-rational (and the pre/trans fallacy)
- idea: Es un modelo de tres momentos que Dan atribuye a Ken Wilber. En la etapa principiante o pre-racional uno se aferra a las enseñanzas condicionadas; incluso una mentalidad anti-Matrix puede ser otra Matrix. En la etapa intermedia o racional, cuestionar se convierte en amargura: callouts, quejas contra "el juego", creencias del tipo "everyone is a copycat". En la etapa post-racional se redescubren los principios desde la experiencia, sin predicar una ideología como la única verdadera. En religión: "bible thumper → atheist → mystic", la trayectoria de Dan (de mormón a ateo y luego a una comprensión más profunda) y la de Jordan Peterson. La pre/trans fallacy consiste en confundir lo pre-racional con lo trans-racional, porque desde el nivel racional ambos parecen no racionales. Lo ilustra el meme de la campana: los extremos coinciden y el medio objeta. Por eso ciertos libros (The Power of Now) suenan "too woo woo" en la etapa racional. La misma secuencia aparece a escala social: premoderno → moderno → posmoderno, junto con la evolución de las herramientas, y hace falta "transcend and include the good parts".
- nivel: avanzado
- evolución: la atribución a Wilber se va afirmando y el vocabulario cambia (ver Evolución)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-010-118
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Your brand goes through beginner, intermediate and advanced stages, and you do different things at each
- **desarrollo:** "Everything goes through developmental stages even your personal brand" (he refers to "the stages of Creator development" in his one-person business roadmap video). To avoid overcomplicating with stages of ego development or spiral dynamics, he simplifies with two examples. First: beginner, intermediate, advanced. You start as a beginner, practice, get results, move to intermediate then advanced. "Beginners won't have validated ideas, you're going to have to test ideas." At the beginner stage you follow a social media growth course. "If you have problems with courses go and sign up for a forty thousand dollar degree and see if you get anywhere in the creator economy": if you want a job, get educated for a job (college); to work in the creator economy, get educated "at a fraction of the cost by people that are in the Creator economy and that literally created it."
- **ejemplos:** $40,000 degree vs creator courses.
- **cita:** "beginners won't have validated ideas you're going to have to test ideas"
- **terminos:** stages of Creator development, beginner intermediate Advanced
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-010-114
- **fuente:** Niche Down Is Terrible Advice (For Smart People).md, 16:49-17:34, 2023-07-04
- **tension:** ninguna

In the middle, mid IQ: "no you have to Niche down, how are you going to land clients if you aren't specific, all the agency owners say you have to pick the most profitable Niche, being a Creator is the same exact thing, you're an idiot if you try to be yourself." On the right, super high IQ: "I do whatever I want."

### U-010-121
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Intermediate (rational) stage: questioning turns into bitterness at the game, callouts and a self-made downward spiral
- **desarrollo:** At the rational stage people question and rebel against the beginner stage. In spirituality this is where atheists come in, "very bitter, they attack and they just argue all day." In the creator domain, "this is where people get bitter at the game." It comes in cycles on the timeline: callouts, drama, calling out scammers ("which isn't a bad thing"), and people who complain all day that the game isn't fair, then lose followers and get sucked into "the downward spiral of their own doing": "I have to write content this way and I'm not going to do it ... why am I not getting engagement, you guys don't know what's good content." Diagnosis: "you haven't accepted the game in the system and how to maneuver yourself within it and you weren't creative enough to turn it into your own thing."
- **ejemplos:** Bitter atheists; creators complaining about engagement.
- **cita:** "you weren't creative enough to turn it into your own thing"

### U-010-123
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Post-rational stage: you rediscover the principles from experience and see the futility of preaching one ideology
- **desarrollo:** "Lastly you evolve to the post-rational stage": you rediscover the principles and approach them from a place of experience, like how most developed spiritual people agree with and embody the principles in the Bible, "or every other pattern across all belief systems that point to truth, because truth isn't limited by cultural understanding," but they "see the futility in preaching it as the one and only ideology like Christianity or else you burn in hell."
- **ejemplos:** Developed spiritual people and the Bible.
- **cita:** "truth isn't limited by cultural understanding"
- **terminos:** post-rational

### U-021-090
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Ken Wilber's pre-rational, rational, post-rational: bible thumper → atheist → mystic (Peterson's and the author's paths)
- **desarrollo:** Another helpful model is Ken Wilber's pre-rational, rational and post-rational, which in religious beliefs can be characterized as "bible thumper to atheist to Mystic." Example: Jordan Peterson, raised in a heavily Christian household, left and became agnostic, and is now "looping back around to God," writing "We Who Wrestle With God," approaching things from a symbolic or metaphorical point of view. The author's own path: raised in a Mormon household, turned atheist after challenging those beliefs, and "looped back around to a deeper understanding of the nature of reality." He mostly doesn't call the Ultimate Reality "God" because the term is too reminiscent of the bible-thumper or pre-rational stage and people misinterpret his beliefs as that; "but if God were the term that's the term I would use."
- **ejemplos:** Jordan Peterson's religious trajectory; the author's Mormon → atheist → deeper understanding trajectory.
- **cita:** "bible thumper to atheist to Mystic"
- **terminos:** pre-rational, rational, post-rational, Ultimate Reality
- **origen:** de-tercero:Ken Wilber
- **nivel:** avanzado

You can't be in the rational stage if you're trying to absorb the teachings of this book because it's just not going to make sense to you. It's going to sound too woo woo." You haven't had those spiritual experiences — "that direct experience to kind of open your mind to this world and the difference between animal and human." The transcript is hesitant about which stages can absorb it (ambiguous).

### U-024-129
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Ken Wilber's pre/trans fallacy: confusing pre-rational (level 1) with trans-rational (level 3) because both look non-rational from level 2
- **desarrollo:** "The pre/trans fallacy by Ken Wilber... occurs when people confuse pre-rational or level one states with trans-rational or level three states, because both appear non-rational from a conventional rational or level two perspective." One can elevate a level-one primitive state to level-three status, and some reduce genuine level-three development to primitive thinking. Example: in religion, level one are "the Bible thumpers" — submit to authority, never question. Level three is mysticism or a more enlightened Christianity (religion/ideology agnostic). Dan Koe's point: most level-three characteristics and principles you live by "are going to also be found in the level one style of thinking. You just don't submit to the authority, and you came to that conclusion on your own." He references the bell-curve-style meme (level 1 and 3 think similarly, level 2 objects) as "the most true meme that has ever came out". Also explains why level-3 people may look narrow-minded: intentional noise filtering.
- **ejemplos:** Bible thumpers vs mystics; the midwit meme.
- **cita:** "you just don't submit to the authority, and you came to that conclusion on your own"
- **terminos:** pre/trans fallacy; pre-rational; trans-rational
- **origen:** de-tercero:Ken Wilber
- **nivel:** avanzado
- **prerrequisitos:** U-024-112
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Reinvent Your Entire Life (In 6-12 Months).md, 28:02-29:47, 13:52, 2025-10-19
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-022-097
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Societies evolve physically (tools) and mentally (worldviews) together: tribes → villages → empires → nation-states; premodern → modern → postmodern.
- **desarrollo:** "They're both deeply intertwined because the tools allow for the worldview or the value system to emerge." Physically: tribes → villages → empires → nation-states. Technology: hunting and gathering with simple tools → digging sticks and hoes (some food surplus) → horse-drawn plow (more surplus, allowing people to "discover and explore and conquer" — "close to the Enlightenment and why that happened") → machines and computers, abstracting us further away from human and animal labor. Mentally (collectives): premodern → modern → postmodern, or pre-rational → rational → post-rational → trans-rational. Early societies conformed to authority ("rain gods in the sky"); then science and reason and valuing progress; now a postmodern world that "deconstruct[s] everything and we think everything should be equal when it doesn't work that way." "We need to transcend and include the good parts."
- **ejemplos:** Rain gods; horse-drawn plow.
- **cita:** "The tools allow for the worldview or the value system to emerge."
- **terminos:** pre-modern; modern; post-modern; pre-rational; rational; post-rational; trans-rational
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Ken Wilber (inferido)
- **nivel:** avanzado
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-094
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [26:08]-[27:38] (2026-02-22)
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T08-03: Stupid thinking (shallow thinking)
- idea: Para entender el buen pensamiento conviene estudiar su opuesto. En 2022 el autor lo llama shallow thinking: tomar lo que ves como ley y creer que la pieza del rompecabezas cósmico que percibes es todo lo que hay (ej.: quien atribuye a la dieta carnívora lo que logró al eliminar comida basura). En 2025 lo define como pensar desde un solo cuadrante (AQAL) y predicarlo como la única vía; en 2026, como "stop thinking too early": pensar un poco, llegar al límite y colapsar en lo que ya sabes, escupiendo pensamientos preprogramados, con cuatro rasgos (unidimensional, reduccionista, tribal, no cuestionador). Conformarse con una sola perspectiva, religión, modelo de negocio o área de estudio "decapita" el pensamiento y es verificablemente no-verdad por su unidimensionalidad. Matiz: todos piensan estúpidamente a diario; no es un insulto sino un patrón.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: shallow thinking (2022) → stupid thinking como pensamiento de un solo cuadrante (2025-07) → stupid thinking como detenerse demasiado pronto, con cuatro rasgos (2026-02). Ver "Evolución y tensiones".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-002
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Shallow thinking is taking what you see as law and believing the piece you perceive is all there is
- **desarrollo:** Before understanding deep thinking, one must understand its opposite. Shallow thinking is "taking what you see as law and never seeing beyond your sensory perceptions", or thinking that what you see is all there is. Using the image of a cosmic puzzle: you see one little piece of the cosmic image and think "that's it", there is nothing beyond it, no depth, no connection, no relationship to other things, nowhere else for the mind to go. So the mind gets stuck, closed off, "closed-minded" to that one thing you are focused on. Narrow focus means not being able to open your mind to the potential of other things, and "that is the problem".
- **ejemplos:** Metaphor of the piece of the cosmic puzzle mistaken for the whole image.
- **cita:** "thinking that a piece of the cosmic puzzle or a little piece of the cosmic image is all that there is"
- **terminos:** shallow thinking; closed-minded; narrow focus
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** The Matrix Is Real (How To Break Free With Your Mind).md, 1:00, 2022-11-20
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-004
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** The carnivore diet example: mistaking one variable change for a universal law
- **desarrollo:** As an example of shallow thinking, the author describes someone who sees a diet fad on social media and takes it as "the only specific way": "oh I cut out junk food and started the carnivore diet so the carnivore diet is the best diet for everyone". But the result wasn't because of carnivore; it was because they cut bad food out of their diet. The error is concluding there is no other option. From this he derives the principle of infinite nuance.
- **ejemplos:** Person who cut junk food and attributes results to the carnivore diet.
- **cita:** "the carnivore diet is the best diet for everyone and it wasn't because I just cut the bad food out of my diet"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-002
- **fuente:** The Matrix Is Real (How To Break Free With Your Mind).md, 3:40, 2022-11-20
- **tension:** ninguna

- **tipo:** heurística
- **titulo:** Thinking tip #1: to understand something (e.g., genius thinking), study its opposite (stupid thinking).
- **desarrollo:** "If you want to understand what something is, like thinking or genius thinking, it helps to understand the opposite, to understand what stupid thinking is." Caveat: "I'm not calling you stupid, I'm not calling you low IQ. I'm simply observing a pattern. Everyone thinks stupidly at times. Everyone thinks stupidly every single day."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno

### U-022-070
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Four traits of stupid thinking: one-dimensional, reductionistic, tribal, non-questioning.
- **desarrollo:** Observed in people who display stupid thinking. (1) One-dimensional: "People try to jam everything into their own perspective and have difficulty seeing outside of it." (2) Reductionistic: "experts in one domain, like business, try to reduce everything to a strategy problem." (3) Tribal: "you only trust your group, your political party, your tribe, your religious people… and you consider everyone else wrong because they don't conform." (4) Does not question: "Your justification for most things is that's just how it's done." As a whole: "stupid thinking is about closing your mind off once you've reached the limits of what you know."
- **ejemplos:** Business experts reducing everything to strategy.
- **cita:** "Stupid thinking is about closing your mind off once you've reached the limits of what you know."
- **terminos:** one-dimensional; reductionistic; tribal
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-069
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [1:37]-[2:31] (2026-02-22)
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-022-071
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Stupid thinking is stopping thinking too early — collapsing in on what you know and spewing pre-programmed thoughts.
- **desarrollo:** "Stupid thinking is when you stop thinking too early. You can still think within your own little bubble, but you never break out of that bubble." Process: you think for a bit, reach the limits of how far you can think, then "collapse in on what you know." Then "you just become this person who just spews out pre-programmed thoughts or what they've been told or what other people believe is right. They don't push the boundaries of their own mind."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "Stupid thinking is when you stop thinking too early."
- **terminos:** collapse in on what you know; pre-programmed thoughts; little bubble
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-070
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [2:31] (2026-02-22)
- **tension:** Matiza la definición de "stupid thinking" del video sobre AQAL (pensar desde un solo cuadrante, U-022-051): aquí el núcleo es dejar de pensar demasiado pronto.


### U-022-051
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** "Stupid thinking" = thinking from only one quadrant and preaching it as the one true way.
- **desarrollo:** "This is actually where stupid thinking comes from and the way that we'll define it, because most people only think from one quadrant and preach that as the one true way." Capitalists focus only on the economic system (LR); scientific materialists on matter and behavior (UR); religions on shared faith (LL); Stoics and Buddhists on the internal world (UL). Wilber's point: "all of these hold truths and they are all overlapping." Locking into one dogmatic worldview "puts you in a box." Danger: "certain problems are best solved or thought about through certain perspectives," and if you can't access the right perspective, the problem won't be solved and "it's just going to cause you unnecessary pain in life."
- **ejemplos:** Capitalists, scientific materialists, religions, Stoics and Buddhists mapped to quadrants.
- **cita:** "Locking yourself into just one dogmatic worldview puts you in a box."
- **terminos:** stupid thinking; dogmatic worldview
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Ken Wilber
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-050
- **fuente:** This mental model will make you think like a strategic genius.md [7:52]-[8:32] (2025-07-20)
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "If you want to become more intelligent you need to expand your mind to see the bigger picture to tell a more holistic story to view situations from all perspectives to not decapitate your thinking by conforming to one perspective one religion one business model one area of study that is verifiably nontruth because of its one-dimensionality."

- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T08-04: Smart but dumb (forcing every problem into one domain's lens)
- idea: El fenómeno "smart but dumb" es el del experto que mete cada problema en su propia lente, como un niño que empuja el círculo en el hueco cuadrado: el hombre de negocios que responde a la depresión con "just do the work", el espiritual que atribuye el fracaso empresarial a una mala vibración. Ambos tienen razón a su manera, pero hay que saber qué forma de pensar aplicar a cada situación; un problema mental no se resuelve necesariamente con un nuevo empleo ni uno espiritual con nutrición, y el dinero rara vez resuelve el sentido (aunque estén conectados). Casos típicos: el fundador que vende por 100 millones y se deprime, el creativo que no sabe cobrar, el "meathead" cuya relación se desmorona. Aferrarse a una sola solución te quita el poder de resolver problemas: trata cada dominio de la vida como proyecto y habilidad en el que coleccionas técnicas.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (la idea aparece en 2023 como consejo práctico y en 2025–2026 con nombre propio, "smart but dumb", y respaldo en cuadrantes).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-073
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** The "smart but dumb" phenomenon: domain experts forcing every problem into their lens (circle into square hole).
- **desarrollo:** "Someone very smart in a domain like business will see someone who is depressed and just attribute that to being a productivity issue": "If you're depressed, just do the work. If you're sad, just do the work." "In some cases that helps, but it doesn't help in every case." Metaphor: a kid trying to mash the circle shape into the square hole. Same on the other side: a spiritual person attributes business failure to "not having the right vibration." "They're both not wrong. They're both correct in their own ways," but you must know how to apply each way of thinking to different situations — a business person benefits from tapping the spiritual lens when a problem is best solved that way. "You probably do the same thing without noticing it"; simply noticing it, and not letting your thinking stop there, "unlocks this new power." Later: polarizing domains (politics, religion, nutrition online) are "a bunch of smart but dumb people just calling each other idiots because they can't see from each other's perspective."
- **ejemplos:** "Just do the work" for depression; "wrong vibration" for business failure; circle/square toy.
- **cita:** "It's like you're a little kid… trying to mash it into the square shape."
- **terminos:** smart but dumb
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-070
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [3:25]-[5:01], [16:40] (2026-02-22)
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-022-076
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Three "smart but dumb" cases: the $100M founder who sells and gets depressed, the creative who can't earn, the meathead whose relationship is in shambles.
- **desarrollo:** (1) A businessman, very successful, makes $100 million, sells the company, and is now depressed — he doesn't know how to solve that "because he's not in business anymore, and that's all he knows." (2) A creative with beautiful work who dedicated their life to their craft but can't make an income "because they don't understand anything in the business domain or even in the psychological domain and how humans perceive value" — they aren't creating something others want. "Not inherently a problem," but if you want to do it full time while working a job, it is: "you're not willing to learn what's going to allow you to do that." (3) A meathead who is fit but whose relationship is in shambles because he doesn't understand social dynamics or "masculine-feminine dynamics"; he got buff to attract a girl but can't keep her, since all he understands is "if I get buff, girls like me."
- **ejemplos:** Listed above.
- **cita:** "Why can't I create art all day?"
- **terminos:** smart but dumb
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-073
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [6:17]-[7:48] (2026-02-22)
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-024-103
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Don't solve a problem with the wrong quadrant: each domain's model causes pain when applied to other domains
- **desarrollo:** "An internal mental problem may not be best solved by vocational means like getting a new job. A spiritual problem may not be best solved by bodily means like nutrition. A capitalist and Christian are developed in their respective domains, but will experience unnecessary pain when trying to apply their model to problems within other domains. Money often doesn't solve for meaning and vice versa, but that doesn't mean they aren't intimately connected." All domains overlap; developing in all of them lets you "become in control of your future".

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-086
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Latching onto one solution strips you of the power to solve problems; treat each life domain as project and skill
- **desarrollo:** For relationship problems you can hire a therapist, watch a YouTube guru, go on a retreat, and experiment until you find the right solution; choosing and identifying with one that may not work is bad. If your business isn't growing, you can buy a course, hire a coach, test a new software, or zoom out and discover new goals by adopting the perspective of life, the universe or your ideal self. "There's always a way to solve your problems and you strip yourself of that power when you latch on to one solution." "Think of each domain of life as both a project and a skill in which you collect techniques to master that domain."

## CLÚSTER C-T08-05: Genius thinking (holding ideas in the realm of possibility)
- idea: El polo positivo del pensamiento recibe varios nombres a lo largo de los años, con un núcleo estable: sostener todo en el "realm of possibility" hasta filtrarlo por experiencia directa. En 2022 es deep thinking (tomar conciencia de los límites que crea la mente, no tomar lo que piensas como ley, cuestionarlo todo; la mente cerrada es su antagonista; "if it is speakable, it is infinitely debatable"); en 2023 es Critical Thinking 101 (dejar que las ideas maduren sin identificarte con ningún bando); en 2025–2026 es genius thinking: la capacidad de sostener ideas amenazantes con intención de entender y no solo de saber, medida por la anchura, profundidad y altura a la que puedes pensar sin quedar cortado; es "your ability to continue thinking" y sintetizar las mejores partes de múltiples perspectivas en una. Se practica en lo pequeño: notar cuándo la mente se siente amenazada, ser honesto y seguir abierto ("thinking in a nutshell"), y cuando te sientes cuestionado, pausar, observar, no colapsar ni responder: "stop the stopping of the thinking".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: renombre deep thinking (2022) → critical thinking 101 (2023) → genius thinking (2025–2026), manteniendo la fórmula "realm of possibility" y añadiendo la intención de entender y la noción de "continue thinking".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-006
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Deep thinking is becoming aware of the limits your mind creates and holding perceptions in the realm of possibility until filtered by direct experience
- **desarrollo:** The author defines deep thinking with several components: (1) "becoming aware of the limits your mind creates", because "that's all humans do, we create limitations, we create distinctions between two different things, that's how we learn"; (2) "holding everything you perceive in the realm of possibility until filtered with direct experience" — he calls this critical thinking, not taking what you think as law; holding it in your head without labeling, judging or attaching whatever meaning you can, until you come into direct experience to "ground that information"; (3) questioning everything, especially thoughts, beliefs and biases that you think are absolutely true without exception. In short, closed-mindedness is the antagonist of deep thinking.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "holding everything you perceive in the realm of possibility until filtered with direct experience"
- **terminos:** deep thinking; realm of possibility; direct experience; critical thinking; closed-mindedness is the antagonist to deep thinking
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-002, U-023-003
- **fuente:** The Matrix Is Real (How To Break Free With Your Mind).md, 3:40-4:32, 2022-11-20
- **tension:** ninguna

- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Practical step 1, Critical Thinking 101: hold all ideas in the realm of possibility until filtered through direct experience.
- **desarrollo:** "Hold all thoughts, ideas and opinions in the realm of possibility until filtered through direct experience." You don't have to prove something right or wrong instantly; you can let things marinate without identifying with any side, regardless of how urgent others make picking a side seem.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "you are allowed to let things marinate in your mind without identifying with any side of the argument"

### U-023-005
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** If it is speakable, it is infinitely debatable
- **desarrollo:** There is "infinite nuance". Anything that can be put into words can be debated endlessly. If you do not open your mind to seeing beyond, or at least hold the nuance in your own head, you fall into shallow thinking and argument. The principle follows from the conditioning argument: since meaning depends on individual interpretation, any spoken claim has endless angles.
- **ejemplos:** The diet fad example preceding it.
- **cita:** "there's infinite Nuance if it is speakable then it is infinitely debatable"
- **terminos:** infinite nuance
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-003

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-022-053
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Actual thinking is a conversation with yourself; stupid thinking is a conversation that turns into an argument; genius thinking synthesizes multiple perspectives.
- **desarrollo:** "Thinking, actual thinking, not overthinking, is a conversation with yourself. Stupid thinking is a conversation that turns into an argument because you latch on to one answer and aren't able to see the bigger picture. Genius thinking is a conversation that accurately covers multiple perspectives and synthesizes the best parts of them into one." The author compares AQAL to the Japanese concept "ikigai" (transcribed "icy") — "what am I interested in, what am I passionate about, what does the world need" — "but on steroids." He estimates "like 90% of people, if not more, struggle to figure out what they want in life."
- **ejemplos:** Ikigai comparison.
- **cita:** "Genius thinking is a conversation that accurately covers multiple perspectives and synthesizes the best parts of them into one."
- **terminos:** actual thinking; overthinking; stupid thinking; genius thinking
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

### U-022-072
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Genius thinking: the ability to hold threatening ideas in the realm of possibility, with the intention to understand rather than just know.
- **desarrollo:** "Genius thinking, as a definition, would be the ability to hold threatening ideas in the realm of possibility paired with the intention to understand rather than just know." Its mark "is illustrated by the width, depth, and height at which you can think without being cut off from venturing further, often due to holding an idea as absolute." "It's your ability to traverse the full universal web of ideas, reality, or all potential knowledge, and pull them together into something coherent or useful, if not something entirely new." Later restated: "genius-level thinking is your ability to continue thinking."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "The ability to hold threatening ideas in the realm of possibility paired with the intention to understand rather than just know."

## CLÚSTER C-T08-07: The true skeptic (becoming an original thinker starts with skepticism)
- idea: El primer paso hacia el pensamiento original es volverse escéptico, lo que exige entrar al "mental gym": salir de la casa cómoda a la que tu mente se adaptó, aunque amenace la estructura que te permite sobrevivir. Si no puedes identificar el momento en que deconstruiste tus creencias y viste lo equivocado que estabas, tienes trabajo pendiente. El método radical: rechazar toda creencia previa que no pensaste desde cero y sostener todas las ideas en el realm of possibility. Un true skeptic es escéptico de su propio escepticismo (quien duda solo de lo ajeno es "blind and dumb", y quien se etiqueta como escéptico ya no lo es). De Sexto Empírico toma que a todo argumento se opone uno igual y lo adapta como "firm beliefs held loosely": actuar con convicción pero pivotar ante mejor información. Práctica: intentar probarte equivocado investigando ambos lados de cada postura hasta el fondo, o estudiar exactamente lo opuesto a tus creencias, esperar la reacción del ego (incluso física) y recorrer seis preguntas.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: el ejercicio de 2023 (estudiar lo opuesto, U-017-093) reaparece en 2025 integrado en un recorrido de escepticismo hacia la originalidad (U-022-162 a U-022-167); sin cambio de posición.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

Original thinking is difficult work; that's why many people don't do it." "You need to step out of the nice cozy home your mind has adapted to and step into the mental gym. But when you first step into the gym it's daunting; you don't want to go; you're afraid of embarrassing yourself; you're threatening the current structure of your mind that allows you to survive, unless that group you belong to encourages you to step in the gym."

I don't care how right you think they are; if you didn't think them through from scratch you can only assume they are wrong." "When I say reject everything I mean everything. You need to hold all ideas in the realm of possibility before you accept them," because "you need a radically open mind if you want to think outside of the box that you didn't know you were in in the first place." "Whether or not what I just said is valid, my mind is actually able to go down that path and discover new things because I rejected and questioned something that is probably true, but by holding that belief loosely I'm able to explore original thought."

### U-022-166
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Sextus Empiricus: "To every account an equal account is opposed" → suspend judgment; the author adapts it as "firm beliefs held loosely."
- **desarrollo:** Quote from Sextus Empiricus, "the OG skeptic," Outlines of Pyrrhonism: "For to every account an equal account is opposed, and from this we think we come to hold no beliefs." In other words, "for every argument there's an equal opposing argument." "You can argue in any direction infinitely and you will never come to a conclusion"; arguments usually end when someone closes their mind and gets strict. Once you understand this "you suspend judgment and cease to hold firm beliefs." The author's adaptation: "that's not to say that you shouldn't act with conviction toward your goals; it simply means that you should pivot or change direction when you're exposed to better information. So rather than not holding firm beliefs, think of the contradiction: firm beliefs held loosely." "There isn't one set thing that I can tell you for how to act… that's up to your own discretion, making mistakes and just learning."

### U-022-167
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Practice skepticism by trying to prove yourself wrong: research both sides of every take, down both rabbit holes.
- **desarrollo:** The author fell into this naturally via his interests (health, nutrition, gym, business). Online takes swing between "this business model or diet (e.g., veganism) is wrong" and "this is the best new model (e.g., the ancestral diet)," which gets popular then fades. Navigating social media, you'll be convinced things are good or bad and form beliefs and decisions based on what you accept. Challenge: "whenever you notice these things you need to research both sides": what's wrong with the ancestral diet, what are its benefits, and "let both ends go down these certain rabbit holes, because you can't just search it on the surface… you're not going to be able to form your own opinion because you don't have enough other opinions to pull from."
- **ejemplos:** Veganism; ancestral diet.
- **cita:** "You don't have enough other opinions to pull from."
- **terminos:** prove yourself wrong; research both sides

- **nivel:** intermedio

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-093
- **tipo:** ejercicio-del-autor
- **titulo:** Pause and question: study the exact opposite of your beliefs, wait for the ego reaction, then walk through six questions.
- **desarrollo:** Practical step 4. Challenge: study the exact opposite of your beliefs — vegan → study carnivore; freelancer → study a creator/personal brand and how they get clients; Christian → study atheists — trying to prove yourself wrong. It's very difficult; you'll have an ego reaction and even "a physical threat response" while reading; become aware of that and how absurd it is. This forces a reaction so you can become aware of it, seek to understand it, improve and overcome it. "Questions are how you deepen understanding." Pick a business, religion or political ideology you disagree with, study it, wait for the reaction, then ask: (1) Is there something I can learn in this situation? (2) What goal are they working to achieve? (3) Does that align with my goals, and does it matter if not? (4) What is the context they're saying this from? (5) What is the essence or lesson of what they're saying? (6) Can I incorporate an aspect of their beliefs into my own? Over time these birth "incredible insights"; those who work through it get results and more peace.
- **ejemplos:** Vegan/carnivore; freelancer/creator; Christian/atheist.
- **cita:** "you will feel physically threatened you will have a physical threat response as you are reading these things become aware of that too"
- **terminos:** pause and question, ego reaction, physical threat response
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-079
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 24:53–26:31, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna



# SECCIÓN 17.2 — Crear la propia filosofía


## CLÚSTER C-T02a-19: Create your own philosophy (truth seeking, conscious experiments, build your own solution)
- idea: En lugar de adoptar una única dieta, modelo de negocio o ideología, hay que forjar un camino propio. Se trata de ser un buscador de verdad que ataca la raíz de los problemas (a menudo metafísica o existencial), autorreflexionar sobre los patrones de la propia vida, estudiar a los grandes y tomar lo mejor de varios, y mirar las decisiones desde el yo ideal como brújula. El autor lo practicó con un experimento consciente en la dirección opuesta (comer frito, salir), que solo funciona con autorreflexión: "you can't find what you enjoy if you don't find what you don't enjoy". Si quieres lograr algo de un modo distinto al de todos, construye la solución en vez de dejar que una creencia limitante te diga que no se puede.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-008-032
- **tipo:** proceso
- **titulo:** Step 2 — Create your own philosophy: be a truth seeker who attacks the root of problems
- **desarrollo:** There is enough shallow advice on making money, getting laid and band-aids for mental health. We need more truth seekers who understand that step-by-step shallow advice looks good outside but is hollow inside, who can attack the root of problems, which is often metaphysical, spiritual or existential — rarely shallow self-help, because self-help came from the philosophical stuff. "The world is desperate for depth," and you can only create depth by forging your own path. How: philosophy is based on experience; self-reflect to notice patterns in your life; study the greats and find patterns in their lives that align with yours; set your mind on an ideal future; view your problems and day-to-day actions from the lens of your ideal self, so you have a compass for decision making. Take the best from multiple greats and make it your own; be open to changing your philosophy when you encounter new or better ways.
- **ejemplos:** See U-008-033.
- **cita:** "the world is desperate for depth and that it you can only create depth by forging your own path"
- **terminos:** create your own philosophy; truth Seekers; root of problems; ideal self; compass for your decision making; study the greats
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-008-028
- **fuente:** The Future Of One-Person Businesses (Take Advantage Now).md, 22:13-24:39, 2023-03-12
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-002-011
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Conscious opposite-direction experiment: deliberately make mistakes to find what works for you
- **desarrollo:** After stopping the dogmatic fitness content, Dan "went in the complete opposite direction just to see what would happen": eating more fried foods, going out a lot more — "a conscious experiment where I knew that I may damage my health." He came "back full circle to just experimenting and finding what works best for you." The condition that makes it work is self-reflection: you must be conscious of the mistakes you're making. If a weekend of drinking too much impacts work and goals, prevents progress, and you don't internalize it and let goals go unachieved, "then yeah that's a major problem." Conclusion: rather than adopting one specific diet, business model, relationship or ideology, experiment with multiple things and figure out how each can be implemented "in a sustainable way that will allow me to reach my goals but not ... take away from other things in life." Don't write anything off for good. "You can't find what you enjoy if you don't find what you don't enjoy."
- **ejemplos:** Fried foods, going out, drinking weekends; "the worst drugs of course like maybe who the knows."
- **cita:** "you can't find what you enjoy if you don't find what you don't enjoy"
- **terminos:** conscious experiment; self-reflection; making mistakes
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-002-010
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [17:56]–[20:40], 2023-03-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-001-008

- **titulo:** If you want to accomplish something, build a solution to accomplish it — stop convincing yourself you can't because you aren't doing it like everyone else.
- **desarrollo:** Connecting back to the opening motto: if you want to accomplish something, build a solution to accomplish it. If you want to start a business without showing your body/face, build a business without showing your body. Stop letting limiting beliefs convince you that you can't do something because you're not doing it like everyone else. From his experience, the highest paid individuals aren't the ones with looks and glamour — those bank on sponsorships and aren't making much money.

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-25: Advice is not starting (maps, not territory)
- idea: "Advice is not starting": la mayoría pide consejo por miedo a la vergüenza y al fracaso que otros atravesaron; si el 99% de los que no tienen éxito piden el 99% de los consejos, ahí hay una lección. Las preguntas suelen ser tan vagas que equivalen a pedir que otro escriba cuatro libros para ahorrarte el ensayo y error ("write your own book, live your own life"). El problema de fondo es no entender el proceso de aprendizaje, que tiene seis pasos (meta consciente, hábitos, gestionar el dolor del cambio de identidad, programar la mente con información relevante, experimentar, y solo al final buscar consejo técnico), porque el consejo "is only as good as it is specific to your situation". Tampoco hay que ser un "advice junkie" que pide "no fluff, pure value": lo que uno percibe como relleno a otro le cambia la vida. Cursos, libros y consejos son mapas, no territorio: "a candle in the distance" que no decide tus pasos. El verdadero aprendizaje es experiencia directa: no tomar el consejo como ley, probarlo y ver varias perspectivas (él cayó en la trampa del "raw liver").
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: dentro del mismo video de 2023 hay dos listas de pasos que no coinciden (la de seis pasos de U-027-125 y la posterior goal → education+effort → experiment → systemize, cuyas piezas están en U-027-140/141/142); se documenta sin resolver.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-027-124
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Asking for advice is not starting; most people ask out of fear of the embarrassment and failure others went through
- **desarrollo:** "How do I start a business / talking to people / pursuing goals?"—"by starting. Advice is not starting." "Asking for advice is making you stupid." Most people ask because they fear the embarrassment and failure that everyone else went through to reach their goals. Advice isn't always bad, but if "the 99% of people that are unsuccessful ask for 99% of the advice," there's a lesson there—there are books upon books on starting a business or getting six-pack abs and 99% still don't have them. 99% of questions are so vague they're impossible to answer—like asking someone to write four books and make a hundred videos so you can avoid all the trial and error they went through. "Write your own book live your own life"; stop doing things that don't result in "experience boosts." You'll never get a truly actionable answer by outsourcing your entire learning journey out of fear of it.
- **ejemplos:** books on business and six-pack abs.
- **cita:** "advice is not starting"
- **terminos:** experience boosts; Outsourcing your entire Learning Journey
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Asking For Advice Is Keeping You Unsuccessful (Here's Why).md, 0:00–0:53, 2023-08-23
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-145
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Don't be an advice junkie: "no fluff, pure value" demands ignore that fluff is what changes lives
- **desarrollo:** For every actionable piece of advice people "drool over" there are a hundred thousand people seeing no results. Stop "masturbating mentally" to actionable, no-fluff advice: you don't understand what the fluff is and why it's important; what one person perceives as fluff another perceives as pure value; often it's the "fluff" that changes lives. "Don't be an advice junkie do what you want and get better at doing it through education and effort." The video's purpose is to frame decision-making, not be maximally actionable.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "don't be an advice junkie"
- **terminos:** advice junkie; fluff; pure value
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-124
- **fuente:** Asking For Advice Is Keeping You Unsuccessful (Here's Why).md, 19:55–20:40, 2023-08-23
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-125
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** The learning process in six steps: conscious goal, habits, manage identity-change pain, program mind, experiment, then seek technical advice
- **desarrollo:** The problem is a lack of understanding of how to learn—people never contemplated the learning process, so they ask the wrong questions from the start. Steps: (1) have a conscious meaningful goal; (2) form the habits that allow the goal to be achieved; (3) manage the pain and emotions that come with identity change; (4) program your mind with information relevant to that goal; (5) experiment with different techniques; (6) seek technical advice when you encounter problems along the way. Advice belongs at the last step and "is only as good as it is specific to your situation." Books, podcasts, articles and social content have great advice but only "0.001%" applies to your exact situation; the millions of bits you perceive now differ next second and from anyone else's experience and interpretation. "Only once you are in a state of pain struggle or challenge can you receive the Insight necessary to transcend to the next level." This process prevents getting "mentally overweight with useless knowledge." (Later in the video a reworked step list appears—U-027-136 to U-027-140.)
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "advice is only as good as it is specific to your situation"
- **terminos:** learning process; mentally overweight; technical advice
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-124
- **fuente:** Asking For Advice Is Keeping You Unsuccessful (Here's Why).md, 0:53–2:28, 2023-08-23
- **tension:** La lista posterior del mismo video (goal → education+effort → experiment → systemize) no coincide exactamente con esta lista de seis pasos.


### U-022-024
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** Courses, books and advice are maps, not territory — a candle in the distance in a dark room.
- **desarrollo:** "Most people try and try and try just to fail and fail and fail because they try to match someone else's map to their territory." Courses, books, podcasts, teachings, advice "are maps, they are guides, not truth." They are useful only if you understand they are "nothing but a candle in the distance that guides you through a dark room; they do not determine the direction of your steps or if you stumble into a pit of snakes." Further reason: you don't have access to someone else's state of mind, how they perceive the world, or their identity.
- **ejemplos:** Candle in a dark room; pit of snakes.
- **cita:** "They are nothing but a candle in the distance that guides you through a dark room."
- **terminos:** map; territory; candle
- **origen:** propia (usa la distinción mapa/territorio sin atribuirla)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-001-031

- **titulo:** True learning is direct experience: don't take advice as law; test it for yourself and see multiple perspectives.
- **desarrollo:** Self-reliance — figuring things out himself — taught him more than any course or college. "True learning is by directly experiencing something, by not taking people's advice as law, testing it for yourself." This applies beyond business: health, any ideology or belief system. Life is "a conglomeration of perspectives and goals" with problems and benefits that form a singular perspective that makes someone believe something; accepting a specific ideology as law closes your mind to opportunity.

## CLÚSTER C-T08-08: Don't take advice as law (create your own philosophy)
- idea: El autor insiste en que ni él ni nadie debe tomarse como ley: los consejos confiados de los "pockets" de internet hay que filtrarlos con mente crítica, contexto y metas, y apegarse a una ideología es la fuente del sufrimiento (lo descubrió exponiéndose al otro lado en debates de salud). La mejor filosofía está anclada en la realidad y es individual: crea la tuya y prueba qué partes de las prescripciones ajenas encajan con tu vida y tu "level of mind". La teoría tiene que encontrarse con la práctica: las acciones deben alinearse con las palabras (¿puede el filósofo dirigir un negocio en crisis?). Conviene separar medio y mensaje para aprender de maestros imperfectos (Wilber, Watts). Para dominar un campo hay que ir a fondo, estudiar todas las perspectivas sin volverse dogmático hasta crear la propia: "truth is a process, not a commandment". Variantes: "do what makes you most curious" para equivocarte y corregir; leer con mente radicalmente abierta, cuestionar y construir sobre las ideas; no aceptar ideologías de negocio desfasadas; y, con Sahil Bloom, pasar todo consejo por tus propios filtros.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (posición constante 2023–2025).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-002-013
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Confident advice in internet pockets must be filtered through a critical mind, context and goals; attachment to ideology is the source of suffering
- **desarrollo:** Dan: a potentially dangerous thing for beginners on social media is "finding those little pockets where people are giving out ... confident advice which is a good thing but you have to approach everything with a critical mind and you have to take into account the context and goals that everyone is working towards." This applies far beyond health. He found himself in "a radical pocket of the internet" (COVID information, masks) and started intentionally exposing himself to the other side (e.g., researching whether seed oils are good for him). This showed him "any side of any argument regardless of Health politics whatever ... can be argued on either side to the end of the world." Picking one side without regard for that "seems to just be the source of suffering": when you attach to an idea or ideology and people challenge it or get results with something else, it threatens you, you feel the need to defend it, and it digs you deeper into the internet pocket. Consequently Dan tries to give holistic, non-dogmatic advice: he likes the one-person business model and Twitter, but prefaces everything with "make sure this is aligned with your goals ... there may be a better option for you," and avoids "petty little arguments online about the best Outreach strategy or the best way to write a tweet." Joy comes "through experimenting and figuring out a solution creating some form of value and then passing it off to others in a non-dogmatic way."
- **ejemplos:** COVID, masks, seed oils; online arguments about outreach strategies and tweet writing.
- **cita:** "when you attach to some form of idea or ideology that is the source of your suffering"
- **terminos:** pockets of the internet; non-dogmatic; holistic advice
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-002-010
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [23:19]–[25:43], 2023-03-16

And in practical life—what if your dog dies, your brother is kidnapped? "Actions must align with words."

## CLÚSTER C-T09b-21: Develop your own writing system
- idea: Puedes usar los sistemas de escritura de otros, pero los sistemas y el "absolute advice" te quitan la capacidad de experimentar y llegar a tus propias conclusiones, "which is what makes a real expert". Principio universal: investigar lo que quieres hacer, reunir perspectivas y frameworks (PAS, AIDA, PASTOR, frameworks sociales), escribir tus pasos (estructura problema-contexto-pasos; bloques de 30 o 60 minutos; mañana o noche), probarlos a diario, y al chocar con un obstáculo buscar una técnica y cambiar el paso. El resultado es un sistema tan eficaz que puedes venderlo o "milk it", y que reduce el gasto de energía mental (lo que tomaba 4 horas pasa a 1–2): "create steps, refine them over time, boom you win".
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna; la tensión interna de U-013-031 (usar sistemas ajenos es bueno / te quita pensar por ti mismo) se resuelve como "úsalos como insumo y construye el tuyo".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-013-031 <= U-001-096 | paso 8 "develop your own writing system" de 2022 repetido en la compilación de 2024 (conservar la mención a 2-Hour Writer)

### U-013-031
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Step 8: develop your own writing system — absolute advice strips you of the ability to figure things out yourself
- **desarrollo:** You can use other people's writing systems; he says that's fine, but "things like systems and absolute advice... strips you of your ability to figure things out for yourself, self-experiment and come to your own conclusions, which is what makes a real expert." Universal principle: research what you want to do, find different perspectives and frameworks, create your own by writing down your steps (e.g., problem-context-step-by-step structure; writing in 30-minute or hour blocks; morning or night), test and experiment to see what works for you, then eventually package the system and tell people why it works for you. He takes pride that his course helps you create your own system.
- **ejemplos:** 30-minute vs. hour blocks; morning vs. night writing.
- **cita:** "it strips you of your ability to figure things out for yourself self-experiment and come to your own conclusions which is what makes a real expert"
- **terminos:** writing system, self-experiment, real expert
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-013-005
- **fuente:** Learn This Skill If You Want To Thrive In The Next 10 Years.md, 26:02-26:54, 2022-08-28
- **tension:** Slight internal ambiguity: he says using others' systems is "great... and this is what I want you to do," then says they strip you of your ability to think for yourself; the resolution appears to be: research them as inputs but build your own.

Universal principle: research what you want to do, find different perspectives, create your own; literally write down steps (e.g., content structure: problem, context, step-by-step; or work habits: 30-minute vs. hour blocks, morning vs. night); test and experiment to see what works; eventually package the system and tell people why it works for you. He notes his 2-Hour Writer helps you create your own system.

### U-002-105
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Step 8 — Develop a writing system: study frameworks, write down a process, test daily, refine at roadblocks
- **desarrollo:** Procedure: (1) Take out a notebook and study writing and social media frameworks — PAS (problem, amplify, solution), AIDA (attention, interest, desire, action), PASTOR (problem, amplify, story, testimony, offer, resolution); social frameworks like DMing people to network, commenting on larger accounts ("just take a basic social media course and you're fine"). (2) Write down a process you can test for writing newsletters, tweets, etc. (3) Test it daily. (4) When you hit a roadblock (e.g., need better ideas), look it up, find a technique, implement it, change the steps. Mechanism: "you're just creating systems ... step-by-step things that you are going to do on a daily basis to do things better"; when a problem comes up "you actually have something you can change and test." Outcome: a system "so impactful and gets you so much results that you can sell it if you want to or you can just milk it." "That's how you get results with anything ... create steps refine them over time boom you win." Refining also decreases "mental energy expenditure": what took 4 hours of writing now takes 1–2 because it's second nature.
- **ejemplos:** PAS, AIDA, PASTOR; 4 hours → 1–2 hours.
- **cita:** "create steps refine them over time boom you win"
- **terminos:** writing system; PAS; AIDA; PASTOR; mental energy expenditure
- **origen:** propia (frameworks PAS, AIDA, PASTOR are standard copywriting formulas, not claimed as his)
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-002-098
- **fuente:** I Made $800,000 In One Year Writing 2 Hours A Day.md, [8:13]–[10:19], 2023-05-05
- **tension:** ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-44: AI that questions you (personal context, life reset and coach prompts)
- idea: "Thinking comes from questioning": la buena forma de usar la IA no es que te diga qué hacer con tu vida sino que te cuestione con cuidado para descubrir lo que no quieres. El autor no quiere que la IA le diga cuál es su pain and gain story porque necesita su contexto personal; con contexto amplio puede conectar cosas que él no veía y acelerar un proceso que normalmente toma meses de conversaciones (prompt "intense" que no se contiene). Prompts concretos: el life reset prompt (te interroga y produce un plan de vida iterable con anti-visión, visión y proyectos), el metatype prompt y la knowledge base de Human 3.0 (un amigo lloró), el coach prompt (fase de entrevista y luego coaching diario con rendición de cuentas, programable en un agente), el deep work accelerator (priorizar, cuantificar, entrar en flow y hacer check-in) y el "cognitive fingerprint" de Max Bernstein para descubrir qué tienes que decir.
- nivel: complementario
- evolución: ninguna; nota de matiz: el coach prompt de 2026 contempla que un agente publique directamente en Twitter tras refinar el post con él, presentado como uso "more tasteful" que pedirle que escriba todo.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T08-06: Questioning (questioning is thinking)
- idea: "Question everything" está tan repetido que su mensaje se diluyó, pero para el autor es probablemente la mejor habilidad de la vida y el motor de su éxito empresarial: cuestionó "niche down", "espera a 10.000 seguidores para monetizar" o "no se puede ganar un millón trabajando 2–4 horas". Cuestionar es pensar, y muy pocos lo hacen (se nota porque hablan como la televisión o las noticias); es el acto simple pero doloroso con el que descubres tu condicionamiento. Ante un efecto que no te gusta, pregunta por qué y ve pelando capas hasta la causa (cause and effect como ley). Las preguntas dictan la calidad de las respuestas y estas la calidad de tu vida: pueden usarse como "attention anchors" que se sostienen en el fondo de la conciencia sin respuesta inmediata.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-007-078
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Lesson 3: Question everything, the driver of Dan's success
- **desarrollo:** "Question everything" has been said so much its message is diluted; it's not novel, so people don't take it seriously. Yet it's probably the best skill in life, especially business, because people take advice as law, don't see results and call it a scam. Questioning drove Dan's success. Examples of advice he questioned: "you have to niche down", "wait to monetize until 10,000 followers", "you can't make a million dollars working two to four hours a day" ("who said?"; just because you can't doesn't mean he can't build something that allows it). Get limiting beliefs out of your head; stay mindful and question everything you've been told, especially what prevents progress.
- **ejemplos:** niche down; 10,000 follower threshold; $1M on 2-4 hours/day
- **cita:** "questioning is the driver of My Success in business"
- **terminos:** question everything; limiting beliefs
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** none
- **fuente:** 11 Lessons From Growing A 7-Figure One Person Business.md, 14:10-16:27, 2022-11-06
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** How do you dig into your mind, become aware of conditioning, reach profound insights? "Through the simple, but often painful, act of questioning." You can tell who doesn't do it by how they speak: "It's like you're talking to a TV or the news or the most popular social media posts." "Questioning is thinking and very few people do it." - **tension:** ninguna


### U-024-020
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Question everything you don't like to trace the effect back to its deeper cause (cause and effect)
- **desarrollo:** The way you become aware of things in general "is to question". If something happens and you don't like it, ask why; don't just let it be unconscious and keep wondering "why is this all happening to me" — it's because you're not aware of what is causing it. Cause and effect is "a law of the universe": you become aware of the effect (which may be negative in your perception), then ask what the cause is. The cause "is much deeper than you will be aware of at first", so you slowly peel back until you reach it, starting by solving surface level problems.

# SECCIÓN 17.4 — Pensar desde el problema


## CLÚSTER C-T08-02: Problem-first thinking (the thinking process to achieve anything)
- idea: Todo pensamiento útil empieza por un problema: identificarlo, entenderlo a fondo, hipotetizar una meta o solución razonable y crear y ejecutar una estrategia. La mayoría fracasa (negocios que no resuelven el problema correcto, relaciones que no ven o no intentan entender el problema) porque se salta esos pasos. El autor pide entrenar la mente para pensar "problem, problem, problem": si te vuelves solucionador de problemas, las metas quedan implícitas; cuando dejas de crecer y de pensar, los problemas se multiplican. Cada falta de resultados es un problema a diagnosticar y no una excusa; los errores son "your light in the dark" porque revelan problemas, que son límites de tu mente, y (Einstein) no se resuelven desde el mismo nivel de mente que los creó. En la vida no tienes la ecuación, solo una conjetura de la respuesta (order of operations); sostener el problema en mente hace que "ceda" (Wilber: all problems are soluble). Heurística práctica: resolver primero el problema similar de otra persona, porque los ajenos se ven simples.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna explícita; ver en "Evolución y tensiones" la convivencia con "problems are only problems if you interpret them as such" (C-T08-17).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-048
- **tipo:** proceso
- **titulo:** The thinking process to achieve anything: identify a problem, understand it deeply, hypothesize a goal/solution, create and execute a strategy.
- **desarrollo:** "If you want to solve a problem or achieve a goal, you need to identify a problem. Then you need to understand it deeply. Then you need to hypothesize a reasonable goal or solution to the problem. And then you need to create and execute a reasonable strategy to achieve the goal." Sounds simple but is "incredibly complex." Most people fail "because they don't understand the situation they're in. They don't identify the problem. They don't see a goal. They don't strategize. That's the entire thinking process to achieve what you want in life." Thinking here is "not to sound book smart" but "so that we can achieve a good life." Over a 10-year timeline, improving thinking lets you "achieve 10 times more than the average person just by learning how to think."
- **ejemplos:** Businesses and relationships (U-022-049).
- **cita:** "We're not talking about thinking to sound books smart here. We're thinking so that we can achieve a good life."
