### U-027-223
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** Order of operations: the goal is the answer, the problem is the equation; in life you don't have the equation, only a guess at the answer
- **desarrollo:** There's an order of operations to achieve anything within reality's bounds. Consider 4 + 6 × (5 + 3 − 2): the answer is the goal; the equation is the problem; you must add, subtract, multiply in a specific order or get the wrong answer. Simple equations like 1 + 1 = 2 are like tying shoes; achieving goals in the unknown (lots of money, a thriving relationship, being more social) are much longer, more complex equations. In life "you don't have the equation you don't even know the answer all you have is a guess for what you want the answer to be" plus the ability to create an equation—knowledge—through experimentation and error correction. That equation is valuable to you and others: "specific knowledge" that can take the form of a product, service, book, or advice to your children, contributing to humanity's progress.
- **ejemplos:** 4 + 6 × (5 + 3 − 2); 1 + 1 = 2.
- **cita:** "all you have is a guess for what you want the answer to be"
- **terminos:** order of operations; equation; specific knowledge; error correction
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-219
- **fuente:** The Fastest Way To Ruin Your Life (Avoid This While You're Young).md, 6:29–8:13, 2025-01-12
- **tension:** ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T09b-22: Start with the pain point ("problem is the inception of gold")
- idea: El autor empieza casi todo con un problema o pain point: "problem is the inception of gold". Mecanismo: el problema enmarca la situación, captura atención, califica al lector (le dice si es para él, "what's in it for me"), abre un curiosity loop y es el comienzo de toda historia, que anticipa una meta y hace que la mente quiera completar los huecos. Como heurística, el 80 % del contenido de un principiante debería empezar con un pain point; cuando pierde el rumbo, el autor entrena su mente para pensar en pain points (idealmente uno que vivió). Aperturas: "Most people...", "If you struggle with...", "The worst...", "If you want..." / "If you don't want...", y dos mini-frameworks: pain point → proceso, o pain point → why/insight. Para encontrar problemas reales hay que salir de la rutina, mirarse al espejo y cavar hasta la raíz ("I don't like how I look" → ... → "I don't want to end up alone"). Aplicado a escala: un artículo por pain point, con tu historia y conexiones entre ideas, y persistir años.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: estable 2024–2025; ver la tensión con el answer-first del pyramid principle en "Evolución y tensiones".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-014-093
- **tipo:** heurística
- **titulo:** Start everything with a pain point or problem — it frames the situation, qualifies the reader, and starts a story
- **desarrollo:** He likes to start every piece with a pain point or problem; if he has one to target, writing flows easily (blocks still happen). Starting with the problem makes it easy to come up with the point and back it up with pain points and illustrations. Mechanism: a pain point frames the situation and qualifies the reader; when someone becomes aware of a pain point, their mind relates it to a goal in their life, sparking a desire to learn more and helping them know if the writing is for them. Those who stay want a solution; if it's good enough, they're more likely to stick around and invest in further value. Humans make sense of the world in stories; stories usually start with a problem that foreshadows a goal or outcome, which makes the mind start filling in the blanks and want to keep reading to see if it was right.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "stories usually start with a problem that foreshadows a goal or outcome"
- **terminos:** pain point, qualifies the reader
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-014-090
- **fuente:** 4 Frameworks To Write Follow-Worthy Content (+ Kortex Template).md, 11:51-13:22, 2024-09-08
- **tension:** ninguna

When people are receptive to an idea, they understand the problem. Videos usually start with the problem because it qualifies whether the video is for the viewer, so they stay or leave.

(the worst skill you can learn is web design). His best content hits a pain point: "problems capture attention, problems open a curiosity loop, problems make it 100 times easier to make the rest of the post impactful." If you don't know what to write, take any idea, think of a pain point, and finishing is simple. Two frameworks: (a) pain point → process (a list of steps to overcome it: "most people suck at writing — do this, this, this"), i.e., bullet-list content; (b) pain point → why / insight (a perspective or novel insight: "most people suck at writing because...").

## CLÚSTER C-T09b-24: Pain and process (P&P / PP / problem and process)
- idea: "Almost everything I write or speak stems from one specific framework": pain and process (o problem and process), el más fácil. Empiezas con un pain point o problema y luego das el proceso para superarlo; sirve para un post, el inicio de una pieza larga o una newsletter o video completos. En un tweet: dolor + proceso + conclusión que "locks in the engagement". En una newsletter: sección 1 (primeros cinco renglones) con el dolor, idealmente tu historia con él; sección 2, una lista de pasos, tips o lecciones con el qué, el porqué y el cómo de cada uno. Se combina con el pyramid principle: "if you were to just follow those two frameworks you would have great writing and speaking".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: el framework aparece con nombre en 2024-09 (U-014-094) y desde 2025 el autor lo presenta como el origen de "casi todo" lo que escribe o dice (U-022-179). Es una formalización de la estructura problema→pasos de 2022 (C-T09b-27).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-179
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** "Pain and process" (problem and process): start with a pain point or problem, then the process to overcome it.
- **desarrollo:** "Almost everything I write or speak stems from one specific framework that I call pain and process… or problem and process; arguably the easiest one. You start with a pain point or a problem and then you go over the process to overcome that thing." He pairs it with the pyramid principle "which helps with persuasive writing or speaking"; "if you were to just follow those two frameworks you would have great writing and speaking if you practiced." Why start with a problem: (1) it captures attention; (2) it qualifies the reader or listener — answers "what's in it for me"; (3) it is the start of any good story — all stories start with a problem or pain point, or open a curiosity loop "because something's wrong"; (4) it frames the ideas that come next, like a story or solution. (A Cortex doc with examples is offered.)
- **ejemplos:** ninguno

### U-008-164
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** The pain and process framework for newsletters
- **desarrollo:** Choose the topic (research YouTube if unsure, or choose a pain point). Section 1, the first two paragraphs / first five lines: state the pain point; to make it easier, use personal experience — when you faced that pain point, walk them through the story and what changed. Section 2: a list — steps one to five (or however many), or tips, quotes, lessons — things that resolve the pain point. For each item, one to two paragraphs covering the what, the why and the how so it's a full point. "That's how you write your first newsletter."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "pain and process pretty simple"
- **terminos:** pain and process framework; the what the why the how
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-008-163
- **fuente:** Build A One-Person Business As A Normal Person (From $0 To $10K).md, 35:44-36:24, 2024-12-01
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-014-094
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Framework 1: PP / P&P (pain and process) — state the pain, show step-by-step how to overcome it
- **desarrollo:** The frameworks increase in difficulty; the first is simplest, usable in social posts, the start of a long-form piece or an entire video/newsletter. P&P ("pain and process"): state the pain point or problem, then show how to overcome it step by step. Example tweet: "If you're tired all the time [pain]: stop scrolling at midnight, fix your shitty nutrition, go on walks, move your body, become obsessed with a new interest [process]. You're tired because you're trapped in a routine that has the outcome of being tired [conclusion]." It's usually wise to wrap a tweet with a conclusion — many skip this — because the last sentence "locks in the engagement" or makes people like, share or comment. He uses "tweets" as synonymous with any social post (his Instagram is all his tweets).
- **ejemplos:** The "if you're tired all the time" tweet.
- **cita:** "you're tired because you're trapped in a routine that has the outcome of being tired"
- **terminos:** PP framework, P&P framework, pain and process
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-014-093
- **fuente:** 4 Frameworks To Write Follow-Worthy Content (+ Kortex Template).md, 13:22-14:48, 2024-09-08
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T13b-13: Problem-first persuasion frameworks (persuasion 101, PAS, selling framework, promotion sequence)
- idea: Todos los frameworks de copy del autor empiezan por el problema. Persuasion 101: hacer consciente a la audiencia de sus metas y problemas, argumentar la importancia de las metas, ilustrar el impacto de los problemas y mostrar beneficios y dolores. PAS para promociones: problem ("if you struggle with..."), amplify, solution. El selling framework para landing pages, promociones y DMs: meta → qué lo impide → tu solución, más la pregunta "¿qué pasa si no lo logras?". Empezar por el problema permite que la gente se autoseleccione y abre la historia en tensión, incluso al pedirle a tu pareja una noche con amigos. Justin Welsh añade una secuencia de promoción completa: probar pericia y dinero ("setting the table"), señalar la complejidad, pintar el resultado, nombrar la brecha y ofrecer un "peek behind the curtain" a precio de compra impulsiva.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (el principio de abrir con el problema es estable de 2023 a 2026).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-002-134

- **titulo:** PAS for promotions: problem ("if you struggle with..."), amplify, solution
- **desarrollo:** "A simple framework for promotions ... you can just practice with now": PAS. Problem: "capture their attention by talking about the problem they are facing that your product solves" — "'if you struggle with' is a great place to start." Amplify: "talking about how the problem impacts their life and why it needs to be solved." Solution: "positioning your offer as the solution to the problem so they click and read more." Placement: plug products, services or newsletter in the comments of your post, in your story, or randomly in your writing (as he did with the book at the start of the video). Study other copywriting frameworks to learn more.
- **ejemplos:** Plugging The Art of Focus at the start of the video.
- **cita:** "if you struggle with is a great place to start"

### U-007-218
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Selling framework: goal -> what stands in the way -> present your solution (plus "what if you don't reach it?")
- **desarrollo:** A practice framework to apply to everything in your brand (landing pages, promotions, DMs), taking the essence of each part. (1) Identify their goals or desired outcome and make it top of mind by talking about it in content, promotions or DMs ("what are you building right now? what's your goal?"). (2) Identify what stands in the way: why aren't people achieving it, why didn't you at the start? Write it in promotions or ask in outreach ("what's preventing you from achieving the goal?"). Dig into the pain point; if the goal is desirable, they become more aware of the problem. (3) With the goal top of mind and the realization they can't reach it themselves, present your solution to the burning problem. Knowing goal and obstacle tells you if they're a fit; if not, leave it alone; if so, "hey man I have this service or product and I think it really helped, do you want to hear more about it?". Add: ask how their life will be impacted if they don't reach the goal; people move when they're really aware of a problem and its impact. You can resonate ("when I didn't have this business it impacted me this way, does that resonate?"), then "this wasn't initially meant to be a pitch but... do you want to hear more?". Example: goal more money; obstacle no time / don't know how to start / no fitting business model; solution: Dan teaches the one-person business model for $500 over two calls.
- **ejemplos:** $500 two-call one-person business consult
- **cita:** "people move when there is a problem in their life that they are aware of like really aware of"
- **terminos:** goals; desired outcome; pain Point; burning problem; fit
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-007-217
- **fuente:** The One Person Business Model 2.0 (Turn Yourself Into A Business).md, 26:53-29:53, 2023-06-25
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-013-242
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Start with the problem: it lets people self-qualify and opens the story in tension — even when asking your wife for a night out
- **desarrollo:** He always starts videos by naming the problem — this one opened "Nobody knows what skill to learn right now." It lets people self-qualify ("do I know what skill to learn?") and it's the start of the story: a novel usually starts in tension, slowly resolved, adventure, conclusion. Learn by noticing it on social media: most posts imply or state a problem. "This is the most powerful thing you can do. It's so simple": start your writing, speaking, post or product with the problem — "frame the situation." Example: convincing your wife you want a night out with the boys — start with a problem ("you haven't had a night to yourself in a long time; you want to watch Grey's Anatomy and have a chill night alone"), then continue the argument.
- **ejemplos:** This video's opening; night out with the boys / Grey's Anatomy.
- **cita:** "just start with the problem in your writing, speaking, post, at the start of your product"
- **terminos:** self-qualify, frame the situation
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-013-241
- **fuente:** Learn This Skill If You Want To Win In The Next 2-3 Years.md, 16:56-17:36, 2026-07-05
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-005-037
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Justin's promotion sequence: set the table (prove expertise, prove money), reveal complexity, paint the outcome, name the gap, offer a peek behind the curtain at an impulse price.
- **desarrollo:** Steps in order: (1) **Prove expertise** — "every single thing that I've written gets 100,000 impressions or more. Like I can prove that I know how to build an audience there." (2) **Prove you've turned it into money** — screenshots of how much the business and products make from LinkedIn. Steps 1-2 are "all just setting the table... all table stakes... Being an expert, having some authority, showing people that you know what you're doing." (3) **Signal complexity** — "let my audience know that the way that I do those things is really complex," and the complex systems "exist inside my product." (4) **Paint the outcome / takeaway** — "what happens when you have 130,000 LinkedIn followers? Well, you get great job opportunities... asked to speak on podcasts... on stage at events like SaaStr. You make money. You can sell products. You can be a thought leader. You can win awards." "Imagine being able to attain all of these things." (5) **Name the gap** — "What's the gap between not having them and having them? Growing an audience, becoming an expert, and having some authority." (6) **Peek behind the curtain** — "Want to do that? Here's a peek behind the curtain. Want to peek behind the curtain fully? Here's how much it costs." (7) **Impulse-buy offer** — the product is $150 ("not necessarily as much of an impulse buy as my first product at $50"), but "with a nice 25 or 30% discount, it becomes something that people can easily pull the trigger on."
- **ejemplos:** Outcomes list for 130k followers; SaaStr; $150 product, $50 first product, 25-30% discount.
- **cita:** "Want to peek behind the curtain fully? Here's how much it costs."
- **terminos:** setting the table; table stakes; peek behind the curtain; takeaway; the gap
- **origen:** de-tercero:Justin Welsh (curtain idea adapted from Eddie Shlainer)
- **nivel:** avanzado
- **prerrequisitos:** U-005-036, U-005-031
- **fuente:** Justin Welsh Shows You How To Start & Grow A One-Person Business.md, 35:47-37:40, 2021-12-20
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T13b-17: The big problem and the desired outcome (transformation sells)
- idea: Los dos primeros elementos de la oferta. El big (burning) problem, lo más importante: dónde está la gente y adónde la llevará no cambiar (negative outcome); debe ser identificable, relevante, importante y respaldado por investigación, y casi siempre cae en salud, riqueza o relaciones (en el levantamiento de pesas, el problema más urgente no es la salud, sino ser percibido como de alto valor). El desired outcome: "the only thing that sells is a transformation", y depende de haber clavado el problema ("a body that commands respect", más un big benefit). El marketing es llevar a alguien del punto A al B mediante una transformación con una solución única, sin compartimentar el medio (Modern Mastery: "work less, earn more, enjoy life"; el "warrior program" como síntesis de fitness, mentalidad y negocio). Se vende el estilo de vida que crea la meta, no la meta. Y se empieza por la superficie antes de la profundidad: "people start in the gym for vanity and stay for the therapy".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (la formulación de 2023 sobre A→B y la de 2025 con elementos numerados son complementarias).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-009-167
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Offer element 1 — the big problem: relatable, relevant, important, research-backed, plus its negative outcome
- **desarrollo:** First and "most important thing that needs to be nailed down": the big burning problem related to the topic. Where are people now and, if they don't change, where will that lead? It's the big problem and its negative outcome; "the problem is the starting point of their transformation." It must be relatable (they can feel and experience it), relevant, important and worth solving, and research-backed: "make sure that you aren't just making up the problem." Most burning problems fall within health, wealth and relationships.
- **ejemplos:** See weightlifting example (next unit).
- **cita:** "The problem is the starting point of their transformation"
- **terminos:** big problem, burning problem, negative outcome
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-166
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 21:03-21:33, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-168
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Weightlifting problem across health, wealth and relationships; the real burning problem is being perceived as high value
- **desarrollo:** For weightlifting: Health — without resistance training you're more likely to develop chronic disease and have difficulty moving as you age. Wealth — if you don't look good, people won't respect you, "because your body reflects your discipline and commitment," so fewer opportunities, promotions and trust from high-value people. Relationships — you lack confidence to talk to a stranger, or feel insecure taking your shirt off around your spouse, leading to a poor sex life and problems elsewhere. "Surprisingly with weightlifting, the most burning problem here isn't the health aspect. The burning problem is being perceived as high value so that you can get what you want out of life and stop feeling so sorry for yourself." Amplifying the negative outcome: lack of social opportunities leads to loneliness, affecting mental health, making discipline harder elsewhere; you get stuck in a job and environment with no idea why.
- **ejemplos:** Weightlifting.
- **cita:** "The burning problem is being perceived as high value so that you can get what you want out of life"
- **terminos:** high value
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-167
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 21:33-23:02, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-170
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Offer element 2 — the desired outcome: transformation sells; it depends on nailing the big problem
- **desarrollo:** "People want a transformation... That's the only thing that sells is a transformation. I'm here. I want to be here. What's going to help me get there? This product." If your product can't help them get there, or doesn't tell them it will, it won't be perceived as valuable. "If you don't nail the big problem, then the desired outcome isn't going to be something desirable because it's based on a problem that isn't that valuable." So take time with marketing: write about the problem a lot on social media, test health, wealth and relationship angles, see what gets picked up.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "That's the only thing that sells is a transformation"
- **terminos:** desired outcome, transformation
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-167
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 23:57-24:50, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-171
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Weightlifting desired outcome: "a body that commands respect," made concrete for men and women, plus a big benefit
- **desarrollo:** If the problem is lack of confidence, insecurity, not wanting to take your shirt off around your spouse, the general desired outcome is "a body that commands respect." Make it relevant, relatable and research-backed: what do people actually want and why? For men: visible abs, a decent jawline, broad shoulders, feeling confident in their skin when talking to others or getting naked. For women ("I can't really speak to this, but I'd assume"): an hourglass figure, a nice butt, visible abs, feeling hot and sexy, "they want their man to fawn over them." To make it more potent, tack on a big benefit: why do men want chiseled abs? Why do women want a big butt? It stems from the target persona; for Dan himself, abs are "mostly to look sick when I take off my shirt."
- **ejemplos:** Abs, jawline, shoulders; hourglass figure.
- **cita:** "the general desired outcome of the weightlifting example... is just having a body that commands respect"
- **terminos:** big benefit
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-170
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 24:50-26:00, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-172
- **tipo:** heurística
- **titulo:** Start on the surface before going into depth: people start the gym for vanity and stay for the therapy
- **desarrollo:** "Esoteric tip": usually start pretty surface level because beginners aren't looking for depth. Spiritual people in Dan's audience (himself included) go deep and get annoyed when people don't appreciate depth — "it's because they haven't even started." "You have to start on the surface before you go into the depth." With fitness, start with superficial things, then introduce depth. "People start in the gym for vanity and stay for the therapy. They don't start for therapy and stay for the vanity. That's most cases." So talk about the abs and the big butt, then ease them into depth: how rewarding tiny gains are, the parallels between life and weightlifting. Depth won't attract many people on the front end.
- **ejemplos:** Gym: vanity then therapy.
- **cita:** "people start in the gym for vanity and stay for the therapy"
- **terminos:** surface before depth, front end
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-171
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 26:00-26:57, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-008-012
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** The essence of marketing is getting people from point A to point B via a transformation, and the middle is not compartmentalized
- **desarrollo:** The essence of marketing is getting people from point A to point B via a transformation. What is in the middle isn't as compartmentalized as something like biology. In Modern Mastery Dan helps people "work less earn more enjoy life"; point B is to live a good life. That is a niche goal: most people don't want that, some do, and even fewer want it from him as a teacher — "people resonate with me that's Niche enough." His process from A to B is not limited to one thing like building a business; it is health, wealth, relationships, everything it takes to create a good life. Limiting yourself to one specific thing limits the value you can give your audience. Later restated: marketing is getting people from point A to point B "with a unique solution."
- **ejemplos:** Modern Mastery: work less, earn more, enjoy life.
- **cita:** "people resonate with me that's Niche enough"
- **terminos:** point A to point B; transformation; Modern Mastery
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-008-010
- **fuente:** The Future Of One-Person Businesses (Take Advantage Now).md, 7:41-8:30 and 30:01, 2023-03-12
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-008-040
- **tipo:** heurística
- **titulo:** Sell the lifestyle the goal creates, not the goal itself
- **desarrollo:** The key is not to focus on the goal itself ("I learned how to create a website") but on the lifestyle it creates. Dan sells a writing course not mainly because people want to be writers, but because, as his landing page explains, writing is cathartic, improved his thinking, helped him think clearly, built an audience that built a business where he can do most of what he wants, or at least knows with 100% certainty he can create that life.
- **ejemplos:** The 2 Hour Writer landing page.
- **cita:** "it's the focus on the lifestyle it creates for you"
- **terminos:** lifestyle
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-008-039
- **fuente:** The Future Of One-Person Businesses (Take Advantage Now).md, 29:19, 2023-03-12
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-008-041
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** The Warrior program: a holistic self-improvement product synthesizing fitness, mindset and business
- **desarrollo:** If you create a self-improvement product like "the warrior program," where point B is living the life of a warrior, what is necessary? Only fitness? No. Only mindset? No. Only business? No. The synthesis could be a culmination of business principles, mindset advice and a fitness program. "What is your way of getting them to point B?" Have fun, get creative.
- **ejemplos:** The warrior program.

# SECCIÓN 17.5 — Saber no es entender


## CLÚSTER C-T02a-30: Horizontal vs. vertical growth
- idea: El crecimiento horizontal expande dentro del nivel actual (aprender, adquirir habilidades y conocimiento). El vertical trasciende a un nivel nuevo tras un periodo de lucha, dolor o conflicto con las propias creencias que obliga a adoptar otra perspectiva. Para subir hay que autoeducarse, adquirir habilidades, no anestesiarse ante el dolor y corregir los errores en vez de suprimirlos. En Human 3.0, las fases son el desarrollo vertical (subir o retroceder) y los rasgos el horizontal: hay que llenar el nivel en ambos ejes para pasar al siguiente.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: 2025-03 (U-022-022) es una distinción general; 2025-10 (U-024-117) se mapea sobre fases y rasgos de Human 3.0. Es un refinamiento.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-022
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Horizontal vs vertical growth.
- **desarrollo:** "There are two types of growth: horizontal and vertical." Horizontal growth is "expanding within the level of development you are in": learning, acquiring skills, acquiring knowledge. Vertical growth is "transcending to a new level after a period of struggle, pain or wrestling with your own beliefs to the point of needing to adopt a new perspective to view the struggle from a new light so that you can overcome it." Key to growth: continuous self-education, skill acquisition, and "not numbing yourself from the pain or problems that spring up." You increase your level of development by making mistakes, realizing them — not ignoring or suppressing them — "leaning into them and self-correcting toward the evolving string of goals you are trying to achieve."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "Vertical growth is transcending to a new level after a period of struggle, pain or wrestling with your own beliefs."
- **terminos:** horizontal growth; vertical growth; self-correcting; evolving string of goals
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-014
- **fuente:** How To Take Back Control Of Your Life With Strategic Thinking.md [14:54]-[16:32] (2025-03-16)
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-024-117
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Vertical vs horizontal development: phases move you up or down levels; traits fill the level horizontally
- **desarrollo:** Phases "represent vertical development, which is moving up a level or regressing back down". Traits are "horizontal development". "You kind of need to fill the space of the level vertically and horizontally so that you can move to the next one."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "you kind of need to fill the space of the level vertically and horizontally"
- **terminos:** vertical development; horizontal development; phases; traits
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-024-101
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Reinvent Your Entire Life (In 6-12 Months).md, 17:57, 2025-10-19
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T02b-36: Horizontal vs vertical development (and what reaching a new stage requires)
- idea: Según la teoría del desarrollo del ego, hay tres direcciones: horizontal (ampliarse dentro de la misma etapa, "filling the cup"), vertical hacia arriba (transformarse hacia una nueva etapa, "expanding the cup") y vertical hacia abajo (regresión). Al agrandarse la taza hace falta más desarrollo horizontal. Para llegar a una etapa nueva, Dan enumera cinco requisitos: tiempo y desarrollo horizontal; apertura a otras perspectivas; un cambio de fondo por medio de educación y conscious conditioning; compromiso con la verdad; y "cybernetic trial and error" con una meta que pertenezca a la etapa nueva. Atribuye a Wilber (con dudas) el hallazgo de que meditar 15 minutos o más al día acelera mucho el paso por las etapas.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-021-082
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Three directions of development: horizontal (filling the cup), vertical up (expanding the cup), vertical down (regression)
- **desarrollo:** In ego development theory: (1) horizontal — "lateral expansion at the same stage," developing new skills and adding information and knowledge; (2) vertical up — "transformation so growth to a new stage or perspective"; (3) vertical down — "temporary or permanent regression due to life circumstances environment stress and disease." Metaphor: horizontal development is "filling one's cup with skill knowledge etc"; vertical development "the thing that we're aiming for a new level of Mind" is "expanding the cup the capacity of the cup" — the water is still there but now you need to fill more, so you need more horizontal development as vertical goes up. That is "increasing and expanding your complexity of self that's becoming a more unique individual."
- **ejemplos:** Filling vs. expanding a cup.
- **cita:** "vertical development ... as expanding the cup the capacity of the cup"
- **terminos:** horizontal development, vertical development, regression, complexity of self
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Susanne Cook-Greuter (ego development theory)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-021-081
- **fuente:** How To Become More Intelligent Than 99% Of People.md, 24:23–25:19, 2024-11-03
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-021-085
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Requirements for reaching a new stage: time and horizontal development, open-mindedness, education and conscious conditioning, seeking truth, cybernetic trial and error
- **desarrollo:** "Reaching new stages of development requires an evolution of your perspective." You need: (1) time, understanding and horizontal development in that stage — new skills, challenges and goals; (2) "awareness of and open-mindedness to explore a perspective different from your own"; (3) "a fundamental change in who you are through education and conscious conditioning"; (4) "a commitment to deeper understanding and seeking truth"; (5) "cybernetic trial and error to have a goal that resides in a new stage of development and solve problems in the way of that goal and self-correct when you realize you didn't solve the right problem."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "cybernetic trial and error to have a goal that resides in a new stage of development"
- **terminos:** conscious conditioning, cybernetic trial and error
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** avanzado
- **prerrequisitos:** U-021-082, U-021-054
- **fuente:** How To Become More Intelligent Than 99% Of People.md, 27:08, 2024-11-03
- **tension:** ninguna

The author calls this "step one to going up": meditate more, practice mindfulness. The author flags his own uncertainty about the source ("I believe it was through research").

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-10: Knowledge vs understanding (everything is awareness until you execute)

"Knowledge without skill is absolutely worthless": hay que llevar lo que está en la mente a algo tangible con las manos ("learn act repeat"). Toda información es "awareness" hasta que se ejecuta, incluso el consejo más accionable; el mínimo es escribir 10 conclusiones (Dickie Bush: tres por podcast) para que la atención quede "almacenada". El problema moderno es la abundancia de información: se acumula teoría sin experiencia directa que la respalde. Educación y esfuerzo van juntos: "action solidifies true knowledge", y al principio se hace mal.

## CLÚSTER C-T08-11: Knowing vs understanding
- idea: Saber es desarrollo horizontal (acumular hechos, memorizar, estudiar el libro de texto); entender es vertical, la sofisticación de tu "cognitive operating system". Se puede saber mucho y entender poco, y quien entiende más de lo que sabe actúa mejor que quien cree saberlo todo; los "smart people" instalan apps nuevas en un sistema operativo viejo que no actualizan. En 2023 el autor lo plantea como concepto vs experiencia: los conceptos permiten saber y la experiencia permite entender; el filósofo de sillón construye una "mental house" de conceptos para evitar la incomodidad de actuar. En The Art of Focus: el conocimiento se construye hacia arriba y el entendimiento se descubre hacia dentro; transferir la conciencia a otra persona busca su esencia, patrones y vínculos acción-resultado, no sus palabras ni su idioma. De ahí que el mundo necesite "holistic synthesizers" que exploren lo desconocido a diario.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: la misma distinción se formula como concept/experience (2023), como upward/inward (2023–2024) y como horizontal/vertical con metáfora de sistema operativo (2026). Sin cambio de fondo.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

Understanding = vertical: "the sophistication of your cognitive operating system." "You can know a lot and understand very little. You can also understand deeply and know relatively little. But if you understand more than you know, you can still act more effectively than the person who thinks they know everything." Why smart people are stuck: they read books, collect knowledge, "but they can't see anything above that. They can't see into other domains."

### U-017-092

- **titulo:** Understanding vs. knowledge: transferring consciousness seeks essence, patterns and action-outcome links, not words or facts; "all understanding is metaphorical."

We don't transfer consciousness to steal someone's knowledge, words or articulation; we want to understand the essence or experience of the situation, note patterns, understand their actions and observe outcomes. Collected over time, decision-making improves "tenfold." Clarification: transferring consciousness into someone who speaks another language won't teach you the language — "we're talking about understanding here not knowledge." Knowledge is built up, "a mental construction"; understanding is "an inherent part of human consciousness"; we seek the essence beyond mere human existence. Example: strength reflects not only in a bodybuilder but in the universe holding up the world via a gravitational constant, the strength of a bear, the strength of a mother — patterns understood through a metaphorical lens. "All understanding is metaphorical."

# SECCIÓN 17.6 — Estrategia


## CLÚSTER C-T08-27: Strategy vs tactics (tactics flow from a superior position)
- idea: "Strategy is positioning, tactics is execution": una estrategia te expone a las tácticas correctas y sin ella eres una marioneta de los marketers de internet. Con Bobby Fischer, "tactics flow from a superior position": estrategia es mejorar tu posición y empeorar la del oponente jugada a jugada hasta que las tácticas aparecen solas (el ejemplo del ajedrez: movimientos aburridos que construyen la horquilla ganadora). Con Lawrence Freedman, la estrategia no es un plan extenso sino la evolución de una idea central en circunstancias cambiantes; con Hormozi, es cómo asignas recursos limitados frente a opciones ilimitadas, y ejecutarla es saltar continuamente entre la visión macro y la acción diaria micro. Los grandes (Steve Jobs, Alejandro Magno) piensan estratégicamente aunque sean "unconsciously competent". En el trabajo de ideas, la calidad de las ideas y la estrategia hacen que la mayor parte del trabajo esté hecho antes de sentarse.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (definiciones complementarias de terceros y propias entre 2025 y 2026).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

A strategy (1) "exposes you to the right tactics that you didn't even know about in the first place or didn't register as an opportunity"; without it, "you're just going to be thrown around like a puppet by internet marketers because they know how to capture your attention and persuade you that something is valuable even if it may not fit your specific situation and your specific mind."

### U-022-109
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** The chess fork: boring positional moves build the arrangement from which the game-winning tactic appears.
- **desarrollo:** Player one spends the early game doing things "that look boring": not attacking, not capturing, just moving pieces to certain squares. "Not all of the squares on that grid are created equal. It's the same with life. A piece in the center of a board can reach many more places than a piece stuck behind a corner." Player one slides a rook onto an open lane; places a bishop on a path pointing at the enemy king; places a knight near the middle. Player two's pieces get in each other's way; their most powerful pieces are trapped behind their own pawns; their king is stuck in the middle "because they never found a safe moment to tuck it away"; "player two was only reacting to player one's moves." Player one moves the knight to attack king and queen at once — a fork. Player two must move the king; player one takes the queen; "essentially game over." Lesson: "any player who got to that exact arrangement of pieces could have played that game-winning move." Player one got there by building up to it; remove any boring step and "the fork… wouldn't exist. The knight would have gotten blocked, and the king would have escaped." "Strategy is the work of making your position better and your opponent's position worse move by move until the gap between the two is so large that tactics start appearing on their own."
- **ejemplos:** Rook on open lane, bishop on king diagonal, centralized knight, king-queen fork.
- **cita:** "Tactics start appearing on their own."
- **terminos:** fork; superior position
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-108
- **fuente:** The Art of Strategic Thinking (How to Become Wildly Successful at Anything You Do).md [4:37]-[6:32] (2026-08-19)
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "Strategy is not a lengthy action plan. It is the evolution of a central idea through continually changing circumstances." The author argues most lives are mediocre "due to a lack of strategic thinking" because most people aren't in the strategist stage — "but you don't have to be there to create a strategy"; you can create a strategy to get to any of those stages. Successful people are highly strategic: they don't let life happen to them, they reject the default path, they choose the game they want to play (both for their ideal life and the games that allow them to get there), and become the person who can win it through skill acquisition and learning.

## CLÚSTER C-T08-29: The seven pillars / principles of strategic thinking
- idea: Framework de siete componentes para diseñar una estrategia de vida, con dos versiones. 2025 (pilares, con plantilla "strategy worksheet"): (1) strategic intent: listar lo que odias, posibilidades y elegir el macro game, porque "your intent frames what you learn"; (2) self-analysis con SWOT; (3) strategic preparation: cambiar la identidad sumergiéndose en un entorno nuevo (todo lo no alineado es distracción); (4) concentration of force: un proyecto de máximo apalancamiento como "vessel for making mistakes" y 3–5 tareas prioritarias; (5) disciplined execution: nueva rutina, 1–2 horas de tareas clave, todo lo demás alimenta el trabajo; (6) adaptability: la estrategia evoluciona con feedback y la experimentación es la única vía; (7) study general principles: de la realidad (The Kybalion) y del oficio (los fundamentos aburridos con taste y estilo). Un "strategic advisor prompt" de IA ayuda a criticar la estrategia. 2026 (principios, extraídos de siglos de guerra, deporte y negocios): visión, research (inteligencia sobre ti y el dominio, como el general con espías), preparation (recursos, brechas, reservas, cubrirse del riesgo), focus/concentration of force (Francia 1940 y los panzer; McKeown), execution, adaptability (la visión no cambia, las tácticas sí; los sistemas se rompen con el tiempo) y study of principles ("the principle of all principles": todo es habilidad, la suerte es apostar sin estrategia; estudiar principios para crear tus propios movimientos es la originalidad). Ninguna estrategia garantiza ganar, pero evita ser arrollado por el oponente, que suele ser uno mismo.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: reformulación 2025 → 2026: "strategic intent" pasa a "vision", "self-analysis/SWOT" a "research", "strategic preparation" (entorno e identidad) a "preparation" (recursos, brechas y riesgo); se mantienen concentration of force, execution, adaptability y study of principles. Ver "Evolución y tensiones".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-022-027
- **tipo:** framework

- **desarrollo:** Ordered framework, filled out in a "strategy worksheet" template: (1) Strategic intent — direction/aim. (2) Self-analysis — SWOT. (3) Strategic preparation — environment immersion to change identity. (4) Concentration of force — choose a project and priority tasks. (5) Disciplined execution — new routine, 1–2 hours of priority tasks. (6) Adaptability — a mental step; strategy evolves with feedback via experimentation. (7) Study general principles — of the strategy and of reality. Steps 6 and 7 are explicitly called non-practical/"mental" steps. Followed by the premortem technique. Each component detailed in U-022-028 to U-022-036.

### U-022-028
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Pillar 1, strategic intent: list what you hate, list possibilities, choose the macro game — to frame what your mind notices.
- **desarrollo:** "You need direction; you need a new goal from which your mind perceives the world." Steps: (1) think of everything you hate about your current life (the job, body, routine, how you feel) — "start somewhere"; (2) think of a few potential possibilities for your future life (starting a business, being fit and energetic, living out your ideal day); "don't dwell on this, write a few things down and continue"; (3) choose the macro game you want to play. Purpose: "create an aim, an overarching filter… this starts to frame your mind to notice the right information in alignment with that intent." You aren't learning skills blindly; "your intent frames what you learn." Connects to the earlier claim that goals filter perception.
- **ejemplos:** Possibilities: starting a business, being fit and energetic, living your ideal day.

### U-022-029
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Pillar 2, self-analysis via SWOT (strengths, weaknesses, opportunities, threats).
- **desarrollo:** You need to understand yourself to work with your strengths and overcome weaknesses, "because your identity clearly isn't serving you here." SWOT questions: Strengths — what comes easy to you, what are your strengths and talents? (If you answer "nothing," "you are lying and already can't see past the reactions your lower self is feeding your mind to spit out like a robot — think harder.") Weaknesses — what are you notoriously bad at and how is it impacting your progress? Opportunities — what can help you overcome weaknesses and leverage strengths (learning skills, courses, meeting new people)? Threats — what threatens your progress (friends inviting you to a party, lack of time, fear of failure)? Condition: you can come back and add to it over time; these won't all come right away.
- **ejemplos:** Threats: friends' party invitations, lack of time, fear of failure.
- **cita:** "If you respond with a cop-out answer of nothing… you are lying."
- **terminos:** SWOT analysis; self analysis
- **origen:** adaptada-de:SWOT analysis (herramienta de estrategia empresarial)

### U-022-031

- **titulo:** Pillar 3, strategic preparation: change identity by immersing yourself in a new environment; anything not aligned with the goal is a distraction.

"You start by immersing yourself in a completely new environment": change the books you read, the information you consume, the people you follow, everything else, to reflect the goal. Open your mind to new perspectives and let them challenge your beliefs of what's possible. Concrete actions: delete most apps on your phone; unfollow most people; buy three new books; choose a podcast to listen to on walks; invest in resources to learn needed skills; have pre-written messages that decline friends' invitations to go out. Principle: "anything that does not align with your goals is by definition a distraction." You need an environment "solely conducive to you achieving your goal."

### U-022-032

- **titulo:** Pillar 4, concentration of force: a project is a vessel for making mistakes; pick the highest-leverage one and 3–5 daily priority tasks.
- **desarrollo:** With self-understanding, direction, no distractions, and readiness to learn, "now we need to focus our energy on the goal." "The only way to actualize a goal is to build a real-world project; that's the only way to learn as well." "A project is a vessel for making mistakes and overcoming those mistakes in a structured way." Steps: write down 3–5 projects you could build to achieve the goal; choose the highest-leverage project with the most advantageous outcomes; map out 3–5 priority tasks you can do daily to progress on it. The author argues social media is "the highest leverage thing right now — that's where you attract people to your work."
- **ejemplos:** Goal: quit the job you hate → projects: build a personal brand, start a weekly newsletter, start a business in general.

### U-022-033

- **titulo:** Pillar 5, disciplined execution: replace your routine; do 3–5 priority tasks for 1–2 hours, preferably in the morning; everything else fuels the work.
- **desarrollo:** "You need to replace your old routine with a new one because you probably fell into your current routine without knowing it, and if your actions create your future you are falling into a future without knowing it." Execute your 3–5 priority, "lever-moving" tasks for 1–2 hours in the morning or 1–2 hours at night. Then "everything you do throughout the day should fuel your work": what you read, scroll, your conversations, going on walks instead of distracting yourself, eating well. "The only thing on your mind should be strategizing how to overcome obstacles of the game" — this becomes seamless after the premortem.

### U-022-130

- **titulo:** Principle 2, research: a general gathers intel before acting — study yourself and the domain before you begin.
- **desarrollo:** "In a war, a general sends out spies, studies the terrain, and learns the enemy's habits and tendencies because you need intel before you act." Self-intel questions: "When was the last time you studied yourself? When was the last time you audited your habits and what kind of life they were compounding into? When was the last time you paid attention to how your mind reacts to silly little things that end up clogging your head space for hours?" "Most people have zero intel on how to start a business, how to build a career, what healthy relationships look like, how to stay lean and healthy, or what the mastery process looks like." They get a spurt of motivation (New Year's resolution), go about it blindly, and fail. For a spirituality practice: study multiple traditions, read the research on techniques, talk to people who've done it for years, follow a range of accounts, understand your own psychological patterns and triggers — don't "grab the first meditation app that shows up on an Instagram ad."

### U-022-133
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Principle 3, preparation: identify your resources and gaps, build reserves, and hedge against risk before rushing into battle.
- **desarrollo:** "You wouldn't just drop into a battlefield without food or logistics or weapons or support." Identify resources and gaps, then build reserves "so eventually you can execute on your strategy." Resources: money, time, knowledge, skills, location, connections, relationships. Lacking money doesn't block strategy: you can still develop skill and knowledge, and the young have time. People past 20, 25, 30 have ingrained habits, bills, responsibilities — little time or money. "If you lack those resources and they're very difficult to get, what other resources can you go after? Where can you leverage yourself?" Before a major change (quitting a job, ending a relationship, starting a venture): build financial runway, decrease expenses, develop the emotional regulation skills you'll need, line up support systems. "You don't just rush into battle… you need to hedge against risk because every goal comes with risk. And as you go deeper and deeper into the hierarchy of goals… the risk only gets greater." E.g., after a one-person business (info product, simple AI product): team? expand? e-commerce? or lifestyle business ("perfectly valid")? Bigger visions need resources "so that the risk of doing so doesn't absolutely destroy you."
- **ejemplos:** Financial runway; one-person business progression.
- **cita:** "You don't just rush into battle."
- **terminos:** preparation; resources; gaps; reserves; hedge against risk; hierarchy of goals; financial runway
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-123
- **fuente:** The Art of Strategic Thinking (How to Become Wildly Successful at Anything You Do).md [28:19]-[30:28] (2026-08-19)
- **tension:** Tensión con U-022-114 (crear presión: mudarse a un departamento que no puedes pagar).


### U-022-137
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Principle 4, focus/concentration of force: all resources at the decisive point — France 1940.
- **desarrollo:** Greg McKeown (Essentialism): "Is there a point at which doing more does not produce more? Is there a point at which doing less, but thinking more, will actually produce better outcomes?" "Concentration of force is the military principle of focusing all of your resources at the decisive point, rather than spreading thin." Example: France 1940 — the French had more tanks and troops and "far more raw resources," but "distributed their armor evenly across the entire front, so every sector had a few tanks. The Germans took every panzer they had and drove them through a single point, which the French thought was impassable, so they barely defended it." "Focus is about where you put what you have, and in life, you only have so much of it at any given time."
- **ejemplos:** France 1940 vs German panzers.
- **cita:** "Focus is about where you put what you have."
- **terminos:** concentration of force; decisive point
- **origen:** de-tercero:Greg McKeown (cita); principio militar
- **nivel:** intermedio

### U-022-144
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Vision and intent don't change; tactics do — systems fall apart over time, so stay in constant experimentation.
- **desarrollo:** Example: you committed to a morning routine, your job schedule changed — "you don't just abandon the routine. That's what most people do." "The vision and intent of your strategy doesn't change, which is becoming, let's say, more disciplined and grounded. That's why you created a morning routine. But the tactics do." If a meditation or business strategy stops working after 6 months, change it: "be in the constant state of experimentation." "Systems fall apart over time. You should expect that." Examples: "the Notion system that you built out 6 months ago and don't use now. Or the Claude skill you downloaded and don't use anymore." "You have to be skilled enough to know when something isn't working." Social media: people say "I posted this and it was supposed to work… What do I do?" — "You adapt. You experiment. You keep trying. This isn't a job. You don't just study and train a repeatable set of tasks. You have to have the entrepreneurial mind, the creative mind… taking in what's going on and interpreting it and then outputting it and then slowly getting better at that entire process."
- **ejemplos:** Morning routine vs changed schedule; abandoned Notion system; unused Claude skill.
- **cita:** "The vision and intent of your strategy doesn't change… But the tactics do."
- **terminos:** constant state of experimentation; entrepreneurial mind; creative mind

## CLÚSTER C-T08-30: The premortem (choosing what not to do)
- idea: Con Michael Porter, "the essence of strategy is choosing what not to do": la mayoría no fracasa por no poder completar su proyecto sino por procesos psicológicos internos que toman el control del foco (parálisis por análisis, perder la visión por opiniones ajenas, falta de habilidades complementarias, inseguridad financiera, burnout sin mecanismo de recuperación, aburrimiento con el trabajo mundano, deseo de algo vistoso de alto estatus). El premortem identifica esos puntos de falla antes de empezar: imaginar un fracaso espectacular (vinculado a la anti-visión), listar causas psicológicas y externas, priorizarlas por amenaza y generar soluciones para cada una (incluso con un asesor de IA). Resultado: conciencia de cómo funciona la mente y un plan claro; "now it's literally on you".
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

Most people's problem "is they just can't start."

- **titulo:** Typical internal failure modes in a project.

- **ejemplos:** Listed above.
- **cita:** ninguna
- **terminos:** paralysis by analysis
- **origen:** propia
